Nigeria tells dollar-stablecoin issuers: go local or lose retail access

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Retail dollar stablecoins need Nigerian issuers, local licenses and token approval. Existing operators need express interim permission during the transition.

Nigeria’s central bank now requires dollar-pegged stablecoins offered to retail customers to be issued by locally incorporated, licensed entities and receive token approval. The rules took effect November 5, 2026, with a conditional 180-day application transition for existing operators. Continued access for foreign-currency stablecoin arrangements during assessment requires express permission from the Central Bank of Nigeria (CBN).

The CBN regulations classify foreign-currency stablecoins as Class 2. Section 6.5 requires a Nigerian-incorporated entity holding a Foreign Currency Issuer License (Class 2), alongside approval of the token itself.

A foreign license, regulatory status or agreement with a Nigerian intermediary cannot substitute for those approvals. The rules reach regulated activities directed at people in Nigeria even when the provider has no physical presence there.

Issuers and service providers face distinct approval requirements. Non-issuance service licenses or authorizations do not permit stablecoin issuance, while issuer applicants must establish a staffed Nigerian office and effective local management before final licensing, including a resident chief compliance officer.

The framework makes a narrow exception to local issuance. Section 11.10.17 allows the CBN to recognize a foreign-issued stablecoin solely for expressly approved wholesale cross-border settlement. That route excludes offerings to Nigerian retail customers, so it does not provide a substitute for the retail licensing requirements.

Approval also has limits on domestic use. Class 2 Token Approval does not authorize settling, pricing or discharging a naira-denominated domestic obligation unless applicable foreign-exchange or other law expressly permits it.

The regulations preserve the Securities and Exchange Commission’s statutory jurisdiction and do not automatically cancel approvals under other Nigerian frameworks. An existing principal license nevertheless does not remove the need for express CBN approval for non-security virtual asset services.

Section 54.1 gives existing operators 180 calendar days from commencement to submit a complete application for the appropriate license or authorization. They may continue only at their existing scale and subject to the CBN’s interim conditions.

Nigeria’s stablecoin transition has earlier obligations. Foreign-currency tokens follow distinct retail and wholesale approval routes.

Full retail access requires a Nigerian Class 2 issuer and Token Approval; existing arrangements have a conditional transition.

CBN may recognize foreign-issued tokens for expressly approved wholesale cross-border use. Nigerian retail offerings are excluded.

Applying is one obligation. Continued Class 2 access still needs express interim permission.

No named provider or token’s approval status is established. Reporting may be required before 60 days; wholesale recognition is discretionary.

Source-bound legal milestones, not observed approvals. Desktop timeline positions 30, 60 and 180 days proportionally from commencement. Mobile milestones use vertical spacing for legibility. Route connectors show required approvals, not a promised sequence or transfer of funds.

No named provider or token’s approval status is established. Reporting may be required before 60 days; wholesale recognition is discretionary.

The transition also requires registration with the bank within 30 days and supervisory reporting within 60 days, or an earlier prescribed period. Anti-money laundering, counter-terrorist financing and counter-proliferation financing requirements apply from commencement.

For existing Class 2 arrangements, the CBN will assess Nigerian incorporation, issuer licensing and service-provider approvals. Continued access during that assessment requires the bank’s express interim permission and does not create a permanent exemption. Submitting an application alone therefore does not establish permission to keep serving the market.

Operators that miss the application deadline must cease operating until the necessary license or authorization is obtained.

BAAJA Remains Fundamentally Flawed Despite Manager’s Amendment

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Senate Must Allow for an Open Amendment Process

Washington, DC — In the continued rush to hold a vote during the lame duck session on the “Bipartisan American Affordability and Jobs Act of 2026”, the senators crafting the bill released a manager’s amendment on Saturday. The Senate has scheduled a procedural vote to begin consideration of the draft legislation tomorrow.

On Friday, Sierra Club issued a statement that it could not support the permitting bill as currently written, outlining fundamental changes that must be made. The manager’s amendment partially addressed these concerns, but major substantive issues remain.

In response, Sierra Club Executive Director Loren Blackford released the following statement:

“The Sierra Club appreciates the improvements made by the manager’s amendment, but the draft legislation remains deeply flawed. The Senate must allow an open amendment process for additional changes to be made.

“Updating and expediting transmission and clean energy permitting is essential to delivering lower costs, reduced air, water, and climate pollution, protecting communities, and expanding the grid for the American people. But rushing legislation that erodes Tribal sovereignty and people’s right to have a say in what is built in their communities, abandons clean water safeguards, and wildlife protections, while further enabling a rogue president that is waging a war on clean energy and our public lands is not an acceptable outcome.

“Senate Democrats have been given a mandate to act decisively to deliver relief on energy bills and level the playing field for clean energy. They can and must fight for a better deal. Unless additional dialogue and changes can happen, the Sierra Club cannot support this legislation.”

The Sierra Club is America’s largest and most influential grassroots environmental organization, with millions of members and supporters. In addition to protecting every person's right to get outdoors and access the healing power of nature, the Sierra Club works to promote clean energy, safeguard the health of our communities, protect wildlife, and preserve our remaining wild places through grassroots activism, public education, lobbying, and legal action. For more information, visit www.sierraclub.org.

Sony Officially Announces New Playstation Handheld

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The PlayStation Portal is getting a Japan-only upgrade

While Sony has largely given up on the handheld market following the death of the now-lamented PlayStation Vita, the company is taking another swing at the PlayStation Portal. Following in Nintendo’s footsteps, Sony will release a Japan-only version of its handheld. The specs are mostly unchanged: the region-locked Portal will have the same 1080p LCD screen and will still be built around remotely accessing a PS5 to play games.

The big difference is that this version will have internal storage. Because of this added hard drive, the region-locked Portal will cost the same as the global model in Japan, 39,980 yen ($252). Like the Japan-exclusive PS5, it's in Midnight Black.

The PlayStation Portal's main selling point is that it lets users remotely stream games from their PS5 while connected to the same Wi-Fi as the console, so the base version doesn’t really need built-in storage. Adding a hard drive suggests the new version may eventually do more than stream; the product description says Sony included it “with an eye towards future functionality.” It’s unclear if that future functionality is for the PlayStation 5 or for the presumed PlayStation 6.

It isn’t surprising that Sony is releasing a Japan-only PlayStation Portal. The company previously took this approach with the PlayStation 5, releasing a region-locked version of the console for the equivalent of $350. Much like Nintendo released a Japan-only version of the Switch 2, Sony did this to make the system more accessible to domestic customers amid the yen’s declining purchasing power.

The PlayStation Portal sold reasonably well despite its seemingly limited use case, moving an estimated 420,000 units in the U.S. alone within its first year. However, critics had mixed reactions. While it was praised for its build and screen quality, many reviews found that even mild connection issues had a huge impact on gameplay. The system’s streaming-only design, combined with a $199.99 price point, made it a fairly niche device.

That said, Sony has improved the system's software over the years, adding better streaming settings for those with beefy home networks.

Perhaps the device's biggest change is that Sony eventually added cloud gaming support, meaning the device was no longer restricted to PS5 owners. This lets users play any game with PS Plus cloud support, as long as they have a PS Plus Premium subscription (which costs $159.99 a year). As hardware prices continue to rise due to an ongoing RAM shortage, console manufacturers may make another significant push for cloud gaming in the near future, with this revived PlayStation Portal being Sony's first major stab.

It takes a certain kind of industry power to set records using the same game twice. According to early estimates, that's exactly what Nintendo has managed with The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake, especially in terms of physical sales.

Christopher Dring of The Game Business has some numbers around the redux of Link's time-traveling battle to save Hyrule. Right off the top, the Ocarina of Time remake is the second biggest launch of a Zelda game in Britain in terms of physical copies.

When you consider other top contenders in the original, Majora's Mask, Wind Waker and Breath of the Wild, that's a lot of Switch 2 game cards. Speaking of Breath of the Wild, this first week is looking around 35% higher than the first seven days of Link's jump onto the Switch, and that's across both that system and the WiiU.

The launch has been massive, and Ocarina of Time is the biggest-selling physical game this year, by quite some distance, according to Dring, outstripping Resident Evil Requiem, Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 4, Crimson Desert, and what else you'd like to consider. A solid performance for the return of a game that's nearly 30 years old.

It trails behind Tears of the Kingdom, but that's to be expected. There were a lot of Switch players by the time that game came out, whereas people were buying the first Switch to play Breath of the Wild, raising the barrier to entry. The fact that Ocarina of Time has managed these numbers on a slower-selling machine makes this all the more noteworthy.

Nintendo and nostalgia is a powerful combination. Now we have to see how Link's efforts fare against GTA 6.

Russell Brand Moved to Florida Months Before British Police Formally Charged Him With Rape

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

British comedian Russell Brand was found guilty of rape and sexual assault and sentenced to over 10 years behind bars—more than a year after he relocated his entire family to Florida to escape "attacks" by the "media, government, and judiciary."

According to the New York Times, a London court found Brand, 51—who was previously married to pop star Katy Perry, and now shares three children with wife Laura Brand—guilty of one count of rape and three counts of sexual assault, and not guilty of two additional counts of rape and one count of attempted indecent assault.

"The total sentence is one of ten years, six months," Judge Joel Bennathan said while handing down the sentence in a London court on Nov. 9.

Following an April 2025 arrest by Metropolitan Police, Brand denied all charges, sharing a video statement on his X account, in which he insisted that he has "never engaged in non-consensual activity," while conceding that he had acted like "a fool" when he was "young and single."

"I've always told you guys that when I was young and single, before I had my wife and family, who were just about a shot over there, my beautiful children, I was a fool, man," he said in the video, which appears to have been shot in Florida.

The alleged offenses are understood to involve six women and took place between 1999 and 2009. Police interviewed Brand about the allegations—which were first brought to light in 2023, when The Sunday Times and Channel 4, two British media outlets, released an expose in which Brand's accusers claimed they had been assaulted or raped by the comedian.

Brand vehemently denied all accusations of "non-consensual activity" at the time.

He is understood to have moved his family from their home in Oxfordshire in favor of a rental property in the Sunshine State, where he is reportedly living in a $2.5 million home in Santa Rosa Beach.

Although he has not revealed when exactly he moved, it has been reported by multiple outlets that he relocated shortly before the 2024 presidential election. Images taken of the comedian show him enjoying public events in the Santa Rosa Beach area as early as October of that year.

The Times reported in 2025 that Brand, his wife, and their three children had been living in a $3.3 million home in the U.K. before their move. It is unclear if they have since sold it.

Brand has also not confirmed whether he is planning to purchase a home in the U.S., where he is currently renting a property.

In the lead-up to his move, the comedian and "Forgetting Sarah Marshall" star had been making efforts to connect with members of now-President Donald Trump's inner circle, interviewing Donald Trump Jr. at his Florida home in 2024 and attending a black-tie dinner at Mar-a-Lago, where he received a Global Defender of Freedom Award.

In April 2025, Brand sat down for an in-depth interview with Fox News host—and newly minted Florida resident—Sean Hannity, during which he opened up about his past addictions and his Christian faith.

Since moving to Santa Rosa Beach, the comedian has reportedly been seen attending church services, as well as yoga sessions, with one local telling The Sun that Brand "always says hello" when they see him.

It is not the first time that Brand has resided in the U.S., having previously owned multiple properties in Los Angeles—including a $6.5 million Mediterranean-style home in the Hollywood Hills, which he shared with Perry, his wife at the time.

Before that, he was reported to have been living in a 1920s abode nearby, which he purchased for $2.2 million in 2013.

Most recently, he snapped up a three-bedroom, five-bathroom Spanish-style property, also in the Hollywood Hills, this time paying $3 million.

The home, which Brand bought in 2019, was built in 1956. The original listing noted that it had since undergone several renovations. He put that property on the market for $3.5 million in summer 2024.

Reformation Steps Into Home Design With Its First Rug Collection

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

After years of dressing its customers, Reformation is turning its attention to their homes. The Los Angeles-based fashion brand has partnered with French rugmaker Les Éditions de Tapis on its first rug collection, bringing the distinctive designs found in Reformation’s stores into residential spaces.

Swan Fan Circle Rug and Boa Dress in Ivory. Photography courtesy of Reformation.

Reformation has long incorporated rugs by Les Éditions de Tapis founder Thomas Coccimiglio into its retail interiors. “His craftsmanship is unparalleled,” says Lauren Caris Cohan, Reformation’s chief creative officer. “Every Les Éditions de Tapis piece is a work of art that somehow feels both luxurious and whimsical.”

The rugs’ playful patterns and unexpected details have become part of the shopping experience, prompting customers to ask where they could purchase them. Those requests eventually led the two brands to develop a collection specifically for the home.

Chintz Rug in Baby Blue. Photography courtesy of Reformation.

Working together, Cohan and Coccimiglio developed nine limited-edition, made-to-order designs that combine the decorative traditions of Parisian interiors with a more relaxed Los Angeles sensibility. The resulting collection carries Reformation’s fashion sensibility into the home without simply translating clothing designs into floor coverings.

Delicate palm trees, swans, and other figurative motifs appear against softly colored backgrounds, often surrounded by decorative borders that recall antique textiles. Rather than filling every available inch with pattern, many of the rugs leave generous areas of open space, allowing the individual illustrations to stand out.

Le Classique Rug in Ivory and Naya Knit Dress in Tan Tiger. Photography courtesy of Reformation.

Each rug begins as a hand-drawn design before being hand-knotted by artisans in India and Nepal. The process brings traditional rug-making techniques to designs that feel decidedly contemporary.

For Cohan, the move into interiors is less about entering a new product category than extending the design language Reformation has developed across its stores and clothing collections. “We are very intentional about crafting physical environments that reflect who we are in subtle, delightful ways,” she explains. “Like our clothing, there is a sense of ease and elegance with a wink of humor.”

Grand Chintz Rug in Dark Wine. Photography courtesy of Reformation.

The Reformation x Les Éditions de Tapis collection is available at thereformation.com, with prices starting at $1,000. All nine designs are available exclusively online, with material swatches on display at select Reformation stores.

Medium is launching a new note-taking app called TK

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

The company is betting that the future of blogging looks a lot like group chats.

The company is betting that the future of blogging looks a lot like group chats.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

For the first time ever, Medium is becoming more than just a blogging platform. The company is launching a new app, called TK, that combines note taking and journaling with something resembling a private social network. The app is meant to get you writing more, and help you share that writing with friends and fans. And it’s part of a much bigger idea about blogging, and a much broader thesis about the state of the internet.

“I don’t think we’ve actually had a launch of anything important since 2017,” Medium CEO Tony Stubblebine tells me over Zoom, a few days ahead of the company’s biggest launch in apparently almost a decade. That was when Medium’s then-CEO, Ev Williams, decided to quit chasing ad dollars and become a subscription business. “It’s clear that the broken system is ad-driven media on the internet,” Williams wrote at the time. “It simply doesn’t serve people. In fact, it’s not designed to.” Medium began charging for access to most content. “We got out of the attention economy,” Stubblebine says now.

The trend Williams saw back then, though, has only accelerated and metastasized. AI companies are hoovering up any content they can get their hands on, legality be damned; search engines and chatbots are sending less and less traffic back to the web. Medium, like most websites on the internet, relies on Google for a huge portion of its traffic — in the time of Google Zero, that’s an existential risk. “When Google started taking away all the traffic,” Stubblebine says, “what I told them is that this hurts us, but it also hurts you, because I’ll just tell you what my product roadmap is going to do: I’m going to switch to private blogging… We’re just going to take everything underground.”

All of which brings us back to TK, an app and network that has no use or need for Google referrals. The idea behind TK is fairly straightforward: It’s a barebones note-taking app, with emphasis on daily journal-style notes and longer-form writing. The app itself is nice enough, like an ultra-minimalist take on Evernote, but it’s the stuff around the writing that has Stubblebine most excited.

For one thing, you can point an AI model at your writing, and use it to help organize or make sense of all the notes you’ve been taking, by searching through your notes or asking for feedback on your writing. “TK” is both the name of the app and a shortcut in it: type the letters (which writers use frequently as a placeholder — they’re easy to find later, since the letters T and K hardly ever appear next to each other otherwise) and you can summon the model to fill in a bit of information for you. Stubblebine refers to “Unix pipes” a few times in our conversation, and his idea is to use the large language models to do some of the grunt work of both creating and managing notes.

Once you’ve written something – and Stubblebine emphasizes that it doesn’t have to be a blog post — you can then share it however you like. You can share it via links or email the way you’d share a Google Doc, and collaborators can write, comment, and leave emoji reactions. You can also add your writing to what TK calls Libraries. Stubblebine calls the Libraries concept “private blogging,” which is a good metaphor. The early Libraries I’ve seen in the app are mostly centered around a topic, and people can contribute their writing on that topic. There’s no public face to any of it; it’s just a blog for and by its authors. Sometimes a few of them, sometimes a few hundred. There are groups sharing their ongoing book projects, swapping recipes, and posting poetry drafts, all for the eyes of only the other people in the group. It’s blogging meets group chats.

This concept of private blogging is a big part of the future of Medium. “Medium is, at some level, performative,” Stubblebine says. “You have to want to share yourself with the world.” But in a post-Google world, he says, that’s essentially impossible. TK doesn’t measure pageviews or reward authors for getting traffic. “This is a community that’s going to give you feedback and encouragement,” Stubblebine says. “It’s not about the drive to be noticed.” Features like this, he says, are coming to the broader Medium platform soon.

TK is now available, for free, via apps for the web, Android, and iOS. (Stubblebine says a native desktop app is also coming soon.) If TK works, though, it could be a crucial new way for Medium to make money. Obviously, Stubblebine says, there are ways to make money from private and paywalled content. That’s the newsletter business. But also, he wonders, what if there’s a way to package up all the best stuff from the Recipe Box library and charge people, or AI agents, for access? “The generic web is getting dumber and dumber,” Stubblebine says. If AI is the future, “it needs to be based on this layer of human intelligence.” And all that intelligence might be better off outside the confines of the Google algorithm.

Man fined hundreds of dollars after giving vape to quokka for photo

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

A man has been fined after a photo showing a vape being held to a quokka’s mouth on an Australian island sparked a police investigation.

The image, which, according to police, was taken on Rottnest Island off the coast of Perth on November 2, circulated on social media and prompted authorities to investigate the incident.

According to 7news, Western Australia Police traced the image to a 29-year-old man, who was subsequently issued a $200 (approximately $130 USD) infringement notice.

“Investigators tracked the image to a 29-year-old man, who was subsequently issued with a Rottnest Island Authority Infringement by police,” authorities said.

The photo appears to show an electronic cigarette being placed against the animal’s mouth, although it remains unclear whether the quokka inhaled any vapor or was simply biting the device.

Quokkas are native to Western Australia, and touching or feeding the animals is prohibited. Visitors who interfere with quokkas can receive an on-the-spot fine of $200, while prosecutions under biodiversity conservation laws can carry penalties of up to $10,000.

Western Australia Premier Roger Cook condemned the image, describing it as “disgusting.”

“Our quokkas are international drawcards for Western Australia and Rottnest Island because of their warm, welcoming nature,” he said.

“Any attempt to put quokkas at risk or harm them should be met with consequences.”

Tyson Foods is restoring beef production at its Texas plant ahead of a planned rancher deal

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

The company expects the Amarillo plant's second shift to be in full operation by summer 2027 and has begun restoring some operations

Tyson Foods is moving forward with plans to reopen the second production shift at its Amarillo, Texas, beef plant ahead of a planned long-term partnership with Allied Beef Company, a consortium of American ranchers.

The company has begun restoring select second-shift operations and expects the shift to reach full operation by summer 2027. Tyson said the reopening will raise output and bring additional jobs to the area. It did not give job figures, according to Bloomberg.

Tyson said it intends to establish the partnership, which would link the company with American ranchers and create "a more economically sustainable model across the supply chain."

"The future of our beef business depends on strong partnerships across the supply chain," Tyson Chief Operating Officer Wes Morris said in a statement. Allied Beef said the planned partnership offers an opportunity to create long-term value for ranchers.

An Allied Beef spokesperson told Bloomberg the company "works across all stages of cattle production, including both multigenerational ranch and feedlot operations." Packers such as Tyson generally get their cattle through feedlots, which in turn obtain them from other ranchers at auction sales, according to Bloomberg.

Tyson scaled the Amarillo plant back to one shift in January, laying off 1,700 workers and trimming its expected daily throughput by upwards of 2,500 head of cattle. The company also closed its Lexington, Nebraska, beef plant this year. Its beef segment has posted an operating loss every quarter since early 2024, according to Bloomberg.

In August, Tyson announced plans to close two beef facilities and pursue the sale of a third. It said it would consolidate its beef business around plants in Dakota City, Nebraska; Holcomb, Kansas; and Amarillo, and would add a second shift at Amarillo as cattle supply allows.

Tyson CEO Donnie King cited U.S. Department of Agriculture data showing limited signs that ranchers are expanding their herds. He said that indicated supply constraints are likely to persist. Tyson projected an adjusted operating loss of $500 million to $650 million for its beef segment in fiscal 2026.

Tyson is scheduled to report fourth-quarter earnings on Nov. 16, according to Bloomberg.

EVs Displace 5 Times More Oil Globally Than They Did 5 Years Ago — Analysis

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Five years on from Glasgow, EVs have proved the single most effective way to curb oil use, says T&E.

At COP26 in Glasgow, twenty-four countries and a group of leading car manufacturers committed to ending the era of fossil-fuel powered vehicles by 2040 “or earlier”. Five years and two energy crises later, oil savings from EVs globally are up fivefold, new T&E analysis shows. EVs are the single most effective way of cutting oil demand, improving energy security and reducing emissions, says T&E.

While battery electric vehicles (BEVs) represent just 4.2% of the global fleet, they now account for 25% of all new car sales worldwide – up from 6.6% five years ago. This surge has pushed oil displacement from 215,000 barrels a day in 2021 to 1.2 million barrels a day today, or roughly 1.2% of global consumption.

At the same time, electric buses now make up 5% of the global fleet, battery electric trucks are at 1% but in some countries exceed 50% of new sales. T&E estimates these vehicles avoid as much as 320,000 barrels a day. As road transport burns close to half of all the world’s oil, transitioning to EVs is crucial in reducing oil dependency. As the global car and truck fleet continues to grow, EVs are the only thing stopping runaway oil demand, warns T&E.

As electrification takes center stage at this year’s COP, current policies put battery electric cars on track to displace roughly 5.8 million barrels of oil a day by 2034 — matching the sustained disruption seen during the 2026 crisis. Accelerating EV adoption will be crucial to preventing future oil shocks, according to T&E.

Antony Froggatt, senior director at T&E, said: “Electric vehicles are the single most effective way of cutting oil demand. Right now the only winners in our oil addiction are fossil fuel giants and petrostates. Places with near 100% sales of EVs like Norway, Denmark and Hong Kong have effectively insulated themselves from future shocks. Five years after Glasgow, EVs stand as one of the clearest victories in global climate history. At this year’s COP, world leaders must set binding electrification targets. Not just for the climate, but for national security.”

BEVs on the road now avoid more than 200 Mt of CO2-equivalent a year. According to T&E, rapid transport electrification is a vital part of the wider energy transition by providing essential grid flexibility and storage. Just as past COPs accelerated the global boom in wind and solar, backing for electric vehicles at COP31 can deliver the same breakthrough, slashing oil demand and driving emissions down.

[1] Displaced oil is estimated from daily per vehicle savings: about 0.019 barrels per car (derived from Ember), 0.2 per truck (IEA) and 0.15 per bus (bottom-up from typical fuel use and mileage). The 2026 supply loss has been estimated at 5.8 million barrels a day. We use IEA fleet projections for 2030 and 2035 fleet sizes to calculate the offset year of the BEV car fleet. Results exclude plug-in hybrids, vans and two- and three-wheelers.

For the chart, global capacity and generation for up to 2024 come from the US EIA. For 2025, we used IRENA capacity additions and Ember generation data. Values for 2026 are T&E estimates based on Chinese installation data to September 2026 and generation on IEA mid-year growth forecasts. Global BEV sales from Ember.

Transport & Environment’s (T&E) vision is a zero-emission mobility system that is affordable and has minimal impacts on our health, climate and environment. Created over 30 years ago, we have shaped some of Europe’s most important environmental laws.

'Crypto Revolution Is Going Strong': Durov Reports 700,000 New Wallets for Telegram

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Telegram CEO Pavel Durov has shared new metrics on the Gram wallet integration in the messenger.

According to Durov, over the last 48 hours alone, users activated more than 700,000 non-custodial Gram wallets.

The screenshot shared by the Telegram CEO shows more than 329,000 new addresses a day. According to GRAM documentation, a wallet is created automatically when a user receives their first token transfer.

700K+ self-custodial wallets created on Telegram in the last 48 hours alone. Adoption spreads virally: a wallet activates the moment someone receives Grams. The crypto revolution is going strong. https://t.co/JPO2KVMip0

The growth comes after a series of changes Telegram made to the TON network earlier this year under Durov's "Make TON Great Again" campaign.

Back in 2020, Durov was forced to kill the original Telegram Open Network project and return more than $1.2 billion to investors after a lawsuit from the US SEC.

Community developers kept the open-source code alive as The Open Network (Toncoin).

This year, Telegram became the largest validator of the network. Besides, Telegram cut TON fees sixfold.

In June, the token was renamed back to its original name, Gram, after a community vote, with 81% of voters in favor. In July, Durov announced that Telegram would integrate a native non-custodial Gram wallet into every Telegram app.

The Telegram CEO described it as "the largest deployment of a non-custodial crypto wallet in history" and promised instant zero-fee transactions for more than 1 billion Telegram users.

According to TON developer Andrew Rogozov, the custodial Wallet in Telegram will stay separate from the new wallet. Now, onboarding happens natively within standard chat windows.

Still, while wallet creation metrics break records, liquidity inside the network is lagging behind with total value locked at $42.01 million, according to DefiLlama.

These hundreds of thousands of new addresses are mostly making microtransactions, such as buying Telegram Premium, paying for in-app ads, or settling small P2P transfers.

Besides, Telegram's validator weight is raising centralization concerns among industry critics. At the same time, the native wallet is still unavailable for US users.

At press time, Gram is trading at $1.05. The token is still more than 87% below its 2024 high of $8.25.

"The crypto revolution is going strong," Durov said.

Fans have also found details on PS5 Pro features for GTA 6

With GTA 6 just days away, preloading has now begun for players in New Zealand. That means that the game's files are now in the hands of the public, who have wasted no time in scouring over the new information to learn as much as possible about Rockstar's upcoming blockbuster. Even though GTA 6's files are encrypted, they have revealed some exciting tidbits.

The most interesting information that surfaced by poring over all 200+ GB of GTA 6 — it's that big, so be prepared to free up space on your console — are references to features such as "joinSession," "launchActivity," and "launchMultiplayerActivity." These have naturally made everyone's ears perk up as Rockstar has not yet officially announced any multiplayer component for GTA 6, despite the continued popularity of GTA Online.

Rockstar is expected to announce multiplayer for GTA 6 sometime after the base game's release, probably in 2027. The newly discovered phrases in the preload files suggest that multiplayer is in fact coming, though we are still left to guess what it will look like until Rockstar reveals it. The preload also suggests that the story and multiplayer elements of GTA 6 will be separated from each other, allowing players to download only the part of the game they want. That's based on the game's story being an individual download within the game's files.

The preloaded files also gave some much-needed insight into what Rockstar has in store for GTA 6 on PS5 Pro. The files appear to reference 120Hz support, which wouldn't be too surprising for a game on the more-powerful version of Sony's console. There are also mentions of multi-frame support, which Sony calls PSSR on the PS5 Pro. So far, Rockstar and Sony have kept quiet on how exactly GTA 6 will play on PS5 Pro.

Microsoft's Azure Linux gets 470-plus security fixes in one almighty patching session

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

A mountain of CVEs, a certificate cleanup, and yet more work for Microsoft's patching teams

Microsoft has unleashed a whopper of an update for Azure Linux, packing more than 470 CVE fixes into a single release as the software industry's ever-growing pile of vulnerabilities shows little sign of shrinking.

Over the weekend, Microsoft rolled out Azure Linux 3.0.2022 with fixes covering everything from the Linux kernel to open source packages and container software. The changelog runs to hundreds of entries, so anyone planning to read the whole thing might want to put the kettle on.

Microsoft's release notes contain more than 470 CVE references, although some vulnerabilities appear multiple times because they affect different packages. So that's not necessarily 470 separate security holes, but it's still an impressive amount of patching for one release.

The fixes span OpenSSL, curl, Kubernetes, Docker, QEMU, and a lengthy list of other packages that underpin modern Linux environments. The generic Linux kernel has been updated to version 6.6.161.1.

There's also been some housekeeping following the eMudhra certificate incident, tracked as CVE-2026-114406. Microsoft has removed or distrusted root certificates that could validate compromised eMudhra intermediate certificates, while adding checks in Python and Go to reject the affected certificates. It has also preserved SSL.com TLS trust where required, presumably to avoid fixing one certificate headache by creating another.

Container infrastructure gets plenty of attention, too, with fixes spanning KubeVirt, containerd, Docker Compose, and other components used to keep cloud workloads running. Several CVEs crop up repeatedly across these packages, helping explain the eye-watering number of entries in the changelog.

The release includes less security-focused changes too, including new Dockerfiles, an OpenVMM patch for confidential computing support, fixes for systemd hangs, and adjustments to GRUB Secure Boot metadata.

The hefty release comes amid an AI-fueled surge in vulnerability discovery that has left developers struggling to keep up with the resulting patch workload. As The Register reported earlier today, Linux creator Linus Torvalds reckons there may finally be signs that the flood is easing, at least in some corners of the kernel.

Torvalds said fixes are now arriving faster than new bug reports in the kernel's eBPF subsystem, although he noted that most other areas haven't reached that point. Whether the wider software industry is anywhere close to catching up is another matter, and Microsoft hasn't said if AI-assisted research contributed to the vulnerabilities addressed in this release.

Azure Linux, formerly known as CBL-Mariner, is Microsoft's own Linux distribution, developed primarily for its cloud infrastructure and services. That makes keeping its many components patched rather more important than the modest version-number bump might suggest.

And judging by the size of this latest changelog, Microsoft's patching teams aren't likely to be putting their feet up anytime soon. ®

How to buy the store in Probably Stolen

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Whether you are tired of dealing with the landlord and his oppressive price hikes or just want to unlock one of the many endings in Probably Stolen, saving enough to buy the store should be among your end-game goals.

But how much does the store cost? And most importantly, how can you unlock the ability to purchase it in the first place? Here’s how to buy the store in Probably Stolen.

You can buy the store in Probably Stolen by amassing any of the sums below before accepting the landlord’s offer at the end of the week. Don’t take the prices as a guarantee, though, as the store will progressively increase in value once you pass the 50-day mark, no matter your difficulty of choice.

If you don’t have the necessary Credits, a Loan Shark will keep his promise and visit your store a few days after you break past the 5.000 mark. He will come with a proposition, allowing you to purchase the store via a down payment and multiple weekly deposits. Upon making all payments, you will receive the documentation and close the deal.

Even if using the Loan Shark might be a good alternative for those looking to get the store early and unlock the Prodigious achievement in Probably Stolen, most of the player base considers it to be a bad deal in the long run, with investing in the right foundations and getting it done in a single payment being the safest option.

That rings even more true considering how, although inviting, all of the weekly payments part of the deal come with heavy interest and usually demand triple the amount of your rent at twice the risk.

Now that you truly own the store, don’t forget to also check out how to unlock all of the game’s remaining endings. We also recommend taking a look at our Eleanor quest guide so you can see her full story through and avoid any tragic mistakes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Calls from ChatGPT and Claude often get filed as direct. This checklist shows how to track AI search as its own call source.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Guide: AI Max Drove 68% Of Retail Invalid Clicks

Before & after charts don't prove SEO results. Here's how to run an SEO split test, read the numbers and report a result your CFO will trust.

A client asked us a question recently that I wish every client asked.

We sent over a few ideas for their product pages, and one came back with a note: “If we ran this as an A/B split test, what metric would we measure it on? What are we actually trying to achieve?”

It’s a fair challenge. It’s also the one SEO has historically been the worst at answering.

For years, the standard answer has been a before-and-after chart. We changed the title tags in March, clicks went up in April, and everyone nodded. But anyone who has presented SEO results to the C-suite knows the follow-up: “How do you know that wasn’t just Google?”

In 2026, that question is harder to dodge than ever. Core updates, seasonality, and AI Overviews can all move your traffic without you touching a thing.

I’ve written this article from the agency side of the table, where proving a result is part of the job. I’ll be covering what proof actually looks like, how an SEO split test works, which method fits the site you have, and how to report a result so it survives the boardroom.

A before-and-after comparison tells you that something changed. It can’t tell you what.

Organic traffic to any group of pages is being pushed around by a lot of things at once:

Then there’s the results page itself. AI Overviews and AI Mode can change how often people click without your ranking moving at all, and Google counts that AI feature data in the overall Search Console performance report.

So the real question isn’t “did traffic go up?” It’s “did traffic go up more than it would have without the change?” That estimate is called the counterfactual, and every credible SEO test is really just a way of building one.

See also: How Do I Know If AI Overviews Are Taking Clicks From My Site And What Can I Do About It? – Ask An SEO

In my experience, a result that holds up in front of a finance team almost always has four things behind it:

That last point is exactly what our client’s question was getting at. If you choose the metric and what counts as a win after you’ve seen the data, you’ll always find a way to call it a win.

It also forces you to decide what the change is actually for. A title tag rewrite should move clicks from search. A layout change designed to help shoppers compare options is really a conversion test first, with search metrics as a guardrail.

The fix is a short brief, written, and shared before anything goes live:

It rarely needs more than a page, and it changes the final conversation from “do we believe you?” to “did it pass the test we agreed on?”

Giulia Panozzo’s recent piece on the mistakes that undermine SEO experiments goes deeper on the design side.

SEO split testing often gets confused with CRO testing, which splits users between two versions of a page. That doesn’t work for SEO.

Google only ranks one version of each URL at a time, so splitting users can’t tell you how a change affects rankings or clicks from search. And showing Googlebot different pages from the ones people see is cloaking, which Google’s guidance on website testing says is against its spam policies “whether you’re running a test or not.”

So an SEO split test splits pages instead of people. You take a group of pages built on the same template, like product, category, or location pages, and divide them into two buckets. The variant pages get the change, and the control pages stay exactly as they are.

Matched buckets. The two groups need to behave the same way before the test starts. Keep pages that compete for the same queries together as one unit, or a winning variant can simply take clicks from its control twin.

Then match units by traffic level and category and randomly assign them. Some platforms automate the balancing, like SearchPilot’s smart bucketing.

A concurrent run. Both groups run over the same dates, so a core update or a holiday spike lands on both at the same time. That doesn’t guarantee they’ll react the same way, since an update can hit some categories harder than others. That’s why the matching and the check below matter.

A forecast. The control group is used to model what the variant pages would have done without the change. The gap between that forecast and what actually happened is your result.

A pre-launch check. Pick a few pretend launch dates in the past, train the model on the data before each one, and see how well it predicts the weeks after, using windows the same length as your test. If it keeps “finding” effects in periods where nothing happened, it won’t be able to tell you what your change did.

Google’s open-source CausalImpact package is the best-known model for building that forecast. SearchPilot started out on it before moving to a neural network model in 2019 so it could pick up smaller uplifts.

Not every site can run a textbook split test. SearchPilot’s rule of thumb is that the section you’re testing should get around 1,000 organic visits per day.

Below that, tests tend to drag on or never reach a clear answer. That doesn’t mean smaller sites can’t test. It means choosing the right design and being honest about how strong the evidence is.

If you’re limited to a pre/post comparison, you can still make it far more defensible:

The tooling follows the same split. Enterprise platforms like SearchPilot and seoClarity’s SEO & AEO Split Tester handle deployment and modeling, SEOTesting.com runs Search Console-based tests for smaller sites, and CausalImpact is free if you have data support in-house. If your CMS already lets you change a subset of pages on a template, you may not need a platform for your first few tests.

A good SEO split test report only needs a handful of numbers.

Uplift against the forecast. The difference between what the variant pages did and what the model expected. It’s your headline, but it should never appear on its own.

The interval. The range of effects the model considers plausible. Bayesian tools call it a credible interval and others a confidence interval, but either way, if it includes zero, the result doesn’t show a positive effect.

Probability of a positive effect. This is the easiest number for non-technical stakeholders to follow, but check what your tool is reporting. CausalImpact’s “probability of a causal effect” doesn’t say which direction, so a confident loss can score as high as a win.

Be careful with framing, too. A 90% probability of a positive effect still leaves a one in ten chance the change did nothing or made things worse.

Revenue or conversions on the tested pages. The number that turns an SEO result into a business case. Revenue is noisier than clicks, so treat it as directional if volumes are small.

Rankings can help explain why clicks moved, but compare them against the control group too. They’re never proof on their own.

See also: Reporting Uncertainty Without Losing Credibility

Google says Search Console’s Search Generative AI performance reports rolled out to all sites on Aug. 31, 2026, although sites with too few AI impressions won’t see one. The reports show how often your pages appeared in AI Overviews and AI Mode, broken down by page.

So you can compare AI feature impressions for your variant and control groups, the same way you would click. If a change lifts clicks but loses AI visibility, or the other way around, that’s worth digging into before you roll out.

There’s no click data yet, so treat it as a supporting signal. And if you’re using rankings as supporting evidence, remember that every link in an AI Overview is given the position of the AI Overview itself, as Google’s John Mueller recently confirmed.

Here’s what a clean test looks like from start to finish. The numbers are illustrative, but this is the shape you want before you face a CFO.

The change. A new title tag and meta description template for product pages, rewritten to lead with what the shopper is searching for instead of the brand name.

The brief. The primary metric is non-branded organic clicks, and conversion rate can’t fall more than 2% relative to control. The smallest lift worth acting on is 5%, and the analysis date is six weeks after launch. The template rolls out only if the 95% interval sits entirely above zero, the central estimate clears 5%, and the guardrail holds.

The split. 400 product pages, clustered so pages competing for the same queries stay together, then matched by traffic level and category and randomly assigned. That gives roughly 200 variant and 200 control pages, and the setup passes the pre-launch check.

The result. At six weeks, variant pages get 14.2% more non-branded organic clicks than the forecast built from the control group, with a 95% credible interval of 8% to 19%. The model puts the probability of a positive effect at over 99%, and the conversion data rules out a drop beyond the 2% guardrail.

The interval first clears zero on day 27. The test still runs to the agreed six-week mark, and the effect holds.

The business outcome. Organic revenue from sessions landing on the variant pages comes in 9.6% above its own forecast. Its interval is much wider than the click result, so it’s reported as directional. Average position on target terms moves from 6.4 to 4.8 while the control group holds steady, which helps explain where the extra clicks came from.

The decision. The template rolls out to the remaining product pages. From then on, you’re monitoring that the gain persists, since there’s no longer a control group to prove it.

Most tests won’t look this tidy. In 2024, SearchPilot re-ran what it called a representative sample of past tests through its newest model, and 29% came back positive at 95% confidence, up from 13% with its previous best model.

So even with a purpose-built model, most of those tests didn’t show a significant win. That doesn’t mean the changes did nothing, but it’s worth knowing before you push something sitewide.

Lead with the decision, not the methodology. Most stakeholders want to know three things: what you’re recommending, how sure you are, and what it’s worth.

A one-page report that covers this usually looks like:

The recommendation: Roll out, roll back, or record it as inconclusive.

The result in one sentence: “Our model estimates the new template lifted non-branded clicks to the tested product pages by between 8% and 19%.”

What it’s worth: Revenue or leads on the tested pages, plus a rollout projection with its assumptions spelled out.

How you tested it: The pages, split, dates, and method, plus anything that happened during the test, like a core update.

For the projection, start from the conservative end of the range, spell out how the remaining pages differ from the tested ones and call it a scenario, not a result. If the full rollout beats a conservative number, you build trust. If it misses an optimistic one, you lose it.

And report the losers. A test log that only contains wins looks like cherry-picking, because it is. Sharing negative and inconclusive results is what makes the wins believable.

Monitor daily to catch implementation problems, but make the call on the planned analysis date, unless your platform uses a method built for continuous monitoring. Calling a test the first time it crosses the line makes it much more likely you’ll roll out a false positive.

Some tools apply changes with JavaScript. Use the URL Inspection tool to confirm the change appears in the rendered HTML, and check the variant pages have been recrawled since it went live.

For title tests, also check what Google is actually showing. Google rewrites title links when it thinks it has a better option, so the title you’re testing isn’t always the one searchers see.

The client who asked what metric we’d measure wasn’t being difficult. They were holding SEO to the same standard as every other channel in their budget.

Split testing won’t make every change a winner. Plenty won’t be. What it does is replace “trust us” with a result your stakeholders can check for themselves.

Write the brief before you start, compare against a fair control, report a range instead of a single number, and tie it back to revenue. Do that consistently, and “how do you know that wasn’t just Google?” becomes a question you can actually answer.

The strategy at Liverpool in recent years has been to target some of the most exciting young players in Europe, and they are looking to do that once again in the upcoming winter window.

Weather Camera Captures 20-Year Timelapse of Skies Above New Zealand

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Weather Camera Captures 20-Year Timelapse of Skies Above New Zealand

A New Zealand weather camera has captured a remarkable 20-year time-lapse showing two decades of changing skies over Lower Hutt — a city close to the capital of Wellington.

Trevor McGavin’s south-facing Lower Hutt weather camera has been recording 640 x 480 pixel images since June 24, 2000.

The timelapse, published by Peter McGavin, is made up of one low-resolution photograph taken at noon every day, played back at 25 frames per second; each second represents 25 days.

The camera was originally installed in the late 1990s to display local weather conditions on McGavin’s website. However, he explains on YouTube that he has been unable to recover any saved images from before 2006, meaning the timelapse covers approximately 20 years rather than the camera’s entire lifespan.

The footage was captured using a 1990s-era StarDot Technologies WinCam Live camera, which records images at 640 x 480 pixels through a serial RS232 interface. Despite being exposed to the elements for decades, the camera is still operating in 2026.

McGavin says the footage has been processed to reduce flicker and restore more natural colors. However, the processing has also contributed to an apparent lack of sunshine in the video, with some viewers questioning why the skies appear persistently cloudy.

He explains that several factors create this impression. The deflickering process blends cloudy frames with sunny ones, while the extremely fast playback makes individual weather changes difficult to distinguish. The camera’s aging CCD sensor has also gradually lost its sensitivity to blue light, making clear skies appear gray. Dirt accumulating inside the lens has further contributed to the hazy appearance.

These effects become more pronounced toward the end of the video. McGavin points out that Lower Hutt actually averages more than 2,000 hours of bright sunshine annually, adding that sharp shadows visible throughout the footage are evidence of sunny conditions.

The timelapse also offers a glimpse of seasonal changes in the Southern Hemisphere, where seasons occur at opposite times of the year compared with the Northern Hemisphere.

Other details are less immediately obvious. The black cylinders visible on the roof are rain gauges, while the trees in the background include native New Zealand species such as totara and pohutukawa, which retain their leaves throughout the year.

Snow is particularly rare in Lower Hutt. McGavin says the only snow to settle on the ground during the 20-year period fell on the afternoon of August 15, 2011, and had disappeared by the following morning.

5 Airlines Operating Both Boeing 787 Dreamliners & Airbus A350s

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Iroshaya & Airlinephoto | Shutterstock, Simple Flying

Operating a widebody long-haul fleet is one of the most capital-intensive endeavors in all of commercial aviation, putting network planners in a position where they need to manage payload capacity, aerodynamic efficiency, and passenger comfort across thousands of global route miles. Many carriers commit exclusively to either Boeing or Airbus for their twin-aisle operations, though an elite group of global network operators actually use dual-fleet strategies incorporating both the 787 Dreamliner and the A350 family.

This list takes a closer look at five major international airlines that successfully operate both modern composite widebody types together, analyzing how carrier network strategies, route lengths, and cabin reconfigurations dictate the division of labor between Seattle's Dreamliner and Toulouse's flagship twin-jet. Drawing on fleet tracking data and operator reports, we explore the distinct operational roles assigned to each aircraft type across these global networks and where both aircraft fit in a much wider fleet strategy.

Singapore Airlines operates Boeing 787-10s primarily on high-density regional routes across Asia alongside one of the largest A350 fleets in global commercial aviation. As noted in Simple Flying analysis, the airline utilizes the stretched Dreamliner variant to maximize passenger capacity on shorter trunk routes where rapid turnaround and high seat count take precedence over ultra-long-range fuel burn.

Complementing its regional Dreamliners, Singapore Airlines deploys its A350-900 fleet on long-haul and ultra-long-range intercontinental sectors connecting Singapore Changi Airport (SIN) to major global hubs. The carrier is actively retrofitting 41 long-haul A350-900 airframes, comprising 34 standard long-haul units and seven ultra-long-range variants, with brand-new first class and business class cabin products to keep its A350 fleet at the forefront of global passenger luxury.

The clear division of labor allows Singapore Airlines to tailor passenger experience and seating density directly to stage length, ensuring maximum operational efficiency across both medium and long-haul networks. Passengers are noticing the quality too, with reviews from outlets such as Prince of Travel highlighting how seamlessly the carrier transitions passengers between its regional Dreamliner services and flagship A350 long-haul operations and how those products stay consistent.

Etihad Airways is an airline that has shown a clear reliance in recent years on the 787 Dreamliner as the foundational core of its entire long-haul and regional widebody network. Fleet data from Planespotters indicates that the carrier flies 37 787-9s and 10 787-10s, making the Dreamliner family its largest widebody group, surpassing its combined twin-aisle types and even exceeding the operational size of its narrowbody Airbus A320 family aircraft.

Alongside its massive Dreamliner roster, Etihad operates the Airbus A350-1000 as part of a broader fleet growth and modernization strategy that also incorporates upcoming deliveries of the Airbus A321LR, the Airbus A330neo, and the Boeing 777-9. With all of these additions, it would be natural to assume that the Dreamliner will see a gradual phasing out; however, the Dreamliner does not seem to be going anywhere, especially as the airline looks to bring in a further 10 787 aircraft, as per Aviation Geeks. With this order, the 787 will still be the backbone of Abu Dhabi's long-haul operations for the foreseeable future.

Deploying both 787s and A350-1000s enables Etihad to scale capacity dynamically across Western European, North American, and Asian corridors, matching aircraft gauge precisely to seasonal passenger demand fluctuations. The airline is looking to add a wide range of new routes over the coming years, headlined by announced routes such as Abu Dhabi International Airport (AUH) to Calgary International Airport (YYC) and new destinations in China such as Shenzhen Bao'an International Airport (SZX).

The carrier already operates the largest two variants of the Dreamliner.

Japan Airlines operates one of the most sophisticated dual-widebody operating strategies in commercial aviation by strategically pairing extensive 787 operations with a high-profile A350 fleet, both of which feature industry-leading cabins. According to fleet reports from Planespotters, Japan Airlines operates 23 787-8s and 22 787-9s alongside 28 A350 airframes split between the smaller -900 and larger -1000 variants. The balanced allocation allows the carrier to optimize payload capacity, seating density, and fuel burn across vastly different operational environments, tailoring aircraft gauge to specific route economics.

The deployment strategy for Japan Airlines' A350 fleet features a distinct structural division between domestic trunk networks and ultra-long-haul international corridors. The carrier uses its A350-900 aircraft primarily on high-capacity domestic routes across Japan, providing for immense passenger volumes between Tokyo Haneda Airport (HND) and regional economic centers with exceptional turnaround efficiency. In stark contrast, the larger A350-1000 is designated as the true international flagship, connecting Tokyo to premier global business capitals such as New York, London, and Paris with cutting-edge private suites and multi-class cabin configurations designed for extreme long-haul comfort.

Simultaneously, the 787 fleet handles a vital supporting role across medium-to-long-range Asian and secondary North American routes. The lighter composite structure of the Dreamliner family allows Japan Airlines to test new international markets with optimized trip costs and lower financial risk. The fleet setup is clearly working incredibly well for Japan Airlines as the carrier is further expanding its dual-fleet capabilities. Following firm commitments made for 20 additional A350-900s configured entirely for international layouts, deliveries scheduled from 2027 will deepen the airline's reliance on both Boeing and Airbus widebodies to future-proof its transpacific network.

British Airways stands out as one of the elite global operators flying every single iteration of the 787 Dreamliner family alongside the A350. Fleet tracking data from Planespotters indicates that British Airways operates 12 787-8s, 18 787-9s, and 14 787-10s, complemented seamlessly by 18 A350-1000 twin-jets. This expansive widebody mix provides network planners with extraordinary flexibility to carefully allocate capacity across an extensive transatlantic and global route network, headed out of London Heathrow Airport (LHR).

The operational division within British Airways is meticulously mapped to passenger demand profiles and stage-length requirements. The smaller 787-8 handles thinner secondary routes where high-frequency scheduling outweighs sheer passenger volume, while the stretched 787-10 and flagship A350-1000 manage high-density trunk routes equipped with the carrier's modern Club Suite business class product. These aircraft operate across highly diverse global corridors, and ultimately are perfect for this type of role. British Airways can ensure that high-yield corporate markets receive top-tier hard products while secondary destinations benefit from optimal trip economics.

Fleet modernization plans continue to evolve across the carrier's network as older quad-jets are permanently retired. As reported by Aerospace Global News, the 787-10 fleet is scheduled to begin replacing Gatwick-based Boeing 777-200ERs starting in 2029, further improving the wider British Airways network outside of Heathrow. The dual fleet strategy is by no means simple to manage, integrating Rolls-Royce Trent engines across its A350s and select Dreamliners alongside General Electric GEnx powerplants on other Dreamliner subvariants. Despite this challenge, British Airways successfully manages complex maintenance synergies and delivers a uniform premium passenger experience worldwide.

The type is relatively rare, but the carriers flying it offer a pleasant onboard experience.

Qatar Airways is home to one of the largest and most balanced dual widebody fleets in commercial aviation, holding the prestigious distinction of being the global launch customer for the Airbus A350 program. According to detailed Planespotters fleet data, Qatar flies 34 A350-900s and 30 A350-1000s alongside 32 787-8s and 29 787-9 Dreamliners, with substantial additional orders pending for both manufacturer types. The massive operational footprint cements Hamad International Airport (DOH) as a critical intercontinental connector linking East and West through massive widebody coverage.

The airline assigns distinct operational roles to each aircraft family based strictly on stage length, payload capacity, and route economics. While Qatar operates a higher volume of individual flights with its 787 fleet across regional Middle Eastern and shorter European sectors, the A350 dominates ultra-long-haul intercontinental corridors and is the aircraft of choice for many of the airline's flagship routes. As per Simple Flying analysis, Qatar Airways deploys its A350 fleet on roughly 17 routes exceeding 4,000 miles (6,437 km), compared to just three for the Dreamliner, making excellent use of the larger passenger payload and greater fuel range of the A350-1000 to connect Doha directly with distant global markets.

This tactical allocation allows Qatar Airways to maximize fuel efficiency and passenger comfort across complex connecting itineraries channeled through Hamad International Airport. Whether offering the award-winning Qsuite business class environment or taking advantage of advanced composite cabin humidity systems, Qatar's synchronized deployment of both the Dreamliner and the A350 really sets the global benchmark for modern dual-fleet widebody management.

Air Canada Cancels This 7-Hour Long-Haul Route To Europe: See Map Now

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Air Canada is the largest airline operating flights between Canada and Europe. It serves more than 40 destinations across the continent, and most of these routes are operated by its mainline fleet. However, the carrier has been making changes to its transatlantic network.

For summer 2027, the Star Alliance founding member had planned to transfer three European routes to its leisure subsidiary, Air Canada Rouge. These included services between Montréal-Trudeau International Airport (YUL) and Keflavik International Airport (KEF) in Iceland, and Toronto Pearson International Airport (YYZ) and João Paulo II Airport (PDL) in Ponta Delgada, Portugal.

Halifax / Stanfield International Airport (YHZ) to Brussels Airport (BRU) in Belgium also features. However, following its latest weekend schedule update, Air Canada has removed the Halifax–Brussels route altogether. This comes as YHZ is losing other transatlantic services as well for summer 2027.

Air Canada launched flights between Halifax and Brussels in June 2026, marking the first time the two cities had been connected by nonstop scheduled services. The route operated three times weekly during the summer season until September 5, using Boeing 737 MAX 8 jets.

Covering approximately 3,074 miles (4,947 km), it was the airline’s third-longest scheduled route operated by the aircraft type in terms of flight duration, with a block time exceeding seven hours in each direction. Until the latest schedule update, Rouge was due to take over the route on June 17, 2027, running through September 5 with the above-mentioned schedule.

A total of 35 departures had been scheduled from the East Coast hub, the same number as in 2026, but these have now been removed. Air Canada will continue to serve Brussels from YUL year-round with daily flights, as well as four-weekly flights from YYZ during the summer season.

This comes as YHZ has lost several other transatlantic services. Back in September, Simple Flying reported that WestJet was dropping two European routes from the airport, namely Copenhagen Airport (CPH) in Denmark and Madrid Barajas Airport (MAD) in Spain (both were introduced in May only).

The airline primarily cited rising jet fuel prices as the reason for withdrawal, although passenger demand and travel patterns also played a part in its decision. For the Denmark route, for instance, it had expected its codeshare agreement with SAS to generate onward connecting traffic across Scandinavia, but relatively few passengers traveled beyond Copenhagen.

Halifax Stanfield International Airport Transatlantic Network in Summer 2027*

Instead, around 62% of passengers reportedly used Copenhagen as their final destination and connected through Halifax to other Canadian cities, including Toronto, Calgary, and Vancouver. Madrid showed a similar pattern, as an estimated 67% of passengers traveling on the route connected through Halifax.

Besides this, Icelandair has also canceled its service between Halifax and Reykjavík, which it had only resumed in 2024. However, the route had seen capacity reductions over the past two years, which suggests that demand was weaker than expected.

The Canadian airport has lost multiple high-profile long-haul services in recent times.

Whether poor route performance was behind the Brussels cut remains unclear, as Air Canada has not confirmed why it removed the flights from its schedule. With that being said, the Star Alliance member is still planning to expand its European operations next summer.

According to aviation analytics company Cirium, both its scheduled flights and seat capacity to Europe will increase compared to summer 2026. From June 1, Rouge will take over the six weekly flights between Montréal and Reykjavik, followed by the three weekly Toronto–Ponta Delgada service from June 17.

The leisure subsidiary will also continue operating five weekly flights between Toronto and Reykjavik, as it did this summer. All three routes will use 737 MAX 8 jets and are scheduled to be the Canadian flag carrier's only European services operated by the type next summer.

These changes are part of a wider fleet restructuring at Air Canada, which is gradually transferring 45 MAX 8 aircraft from its mainline fleet to its leisure subsidiary. As part of the transition, Rouge is retiring its aging Airbus A319s and transferring its newer Airbus A320 and A321 aircraft to the mainline operation.

Jackie Chan’s ‘Lethal Weapon’ Replacement Officially Takes Over Streaming

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Erielle Sudario is a Collider News and Feature Author from Australia and has worked in the journalism industry since 2018. She has a passion for entertainment and pop culture news and has interviewed YouTubers, voice actors, film directors, and musicians throughout her career. When she's not writing, you can see Erielle building keyboards, reading a good book, playing video games, and creating content on social media.

One of Jackie Chan's most iconic films has recently become a streaming hit. The Hong Kong action star has enjoyed a long film career, beginning in 1959 and appearing in numerous movies and notable franchises. With one of those franchises rumored to return, audiences have begun revisiting the movie that started it all.

The franchise is Rush Hour, a buddy-cop comedy trilogy that debuted in 1998 and the first film grossed over $245.3 million worldwide. The film launched a trilogy starring Chris Tucker and Jackie Chan as two detectives who tackle criminal cases while navigating their cultural differences. Plans for a fourth Rush Hour movie have circulated for years, and Chan has expressed interest in returning if the script is good. However, in 2025, U.S. President Donald Trump reportedly pushed to revive the franchise, with controversial filmmaker Brett Ratner attached to direct the project. So far, few updates have emerged about the fourth installment.

Recently, the first Rush Hour movie found success on streaming, landing at No. 9 on Netflix's Top 10 Movies chart, between Sniper: The Last Stand and The Birthday Gift. The film stars Tucker as LAPD Detective James Carter, who teams up with Chief Inspector Lee (Chan) to investigate a kidnapping case that threatens to become an international incident. Since its release, the film has received praise from audiences and critics, earning a 64% critics' score and a 79% audience score on Rotten Tomatoes.

The first Rush Hour movie was a box-office success, grossing $245.3 million worldwide against its reported $35 million budget and earning positive reviews from audiences and critics. Its success led to the release of Rush Hour 2 in 2001, and it earned more at the box office than its predecessor. The sequel follows the duo as they investigate a bombing at a U.S. Consulate in Hong Kong that kills two agents. The film grossed $347.4 million worldwide and was well received by audiences, although critics were less enthusiastic.

In 2007, the franchise returned with Rush Hour 3, which grossed over $258 million worldwide and received negative reviews from critics, earning an 18% critics' score, but was praised by audiences with a 63% score on Rotten Tomatoes. The third installment follows the two detectives as they investigate a criminal conspiracy in Paris. Altogether, the Rush Hour trilogy has grossed over $850 million worldwide. Out of the three films, Rush Hour 2 also received recognition during awards season, winning several awards, including "Favorite Movie," "Favorite Movie Actor" for Chris Tucker, and "Favorite Male Butt Kicker" for Chan at the 2002 Kids' Choice Awards, as well as "Best Fight" at the 2002 MTV Movie Awards.

Rush Hour is available to stream on Paramount+. Follow Collider for more updates.

A loyal and dedicated Hong Kong Inspector teams up with a reckless and loudmouthed L.A.P.D. detective to rescue the Chinese Consul's kidnapped daughter, while trying to arrest a dangerous crime lord along the way.

The show that claimed the most of your answers is the world you were built for. If two tied, both are shown — you're complicated enough to straddle two Sheridan universes.

You are a Dutton — or you might as well be. You understand that some things are worth protecting at any cost, and that the modern world's indifference to history, to land, to legacy, is not something you're willing to accept quietly. You lead from the front, you carry your family's weight without complaint, and when someone threatens what's yours, you don't escalate — you finish it. You're not cruel. But you are absolute. In Yellowstone's world, that combination of ferocity and loyalty doesn't make you a villain. It makes you the only thing standing between everything that matters and everyone who wants to take it.

You thrive in the chaos of high-stakes negotiation, where the money is enormous, the margins are thin, and the wrong word in the wrong room can cost everyone everything. You're a fixer — the person called when a situation is already on fire and needs someone with the nerve to walk into it. West Texas oil country rewards exactly what you are: sharp, adaptable, unsentimental, and absolutely clear-eyed about what people want and what they'll do to get it. You're not naive enough to think this world is fair. You're smart enough to be the one deciding who it's fair to.

You are a Dwight Manfredi — someone who has served their time, paid their dues, and arrived somewhere unexpected with nothing but their reputation and their wits. You adapt without losing yourself. You build loyalty through respect rather than fear, though you're not above reminding people that the two aren't mutually exclusive. Tulsa King is for people who are still standing when everyone assumed they'd be finished — who find, in an unfamiliar place, that they're more capable than the world gave them credit for. You don't need a throne. You build one, wherever you happen to land.

You carry the weight of a system that is broken by design, and you do it anyway — because someone has to, and because you're the only one positioned to do it without the whole thing collapsing. Mike McLusky's world is for people who are comfortable operating where there are no good options, only less catastrophic ones. You speak every language: law enforcement, criminal, political, human. That fluency makes you invaluable and it makes you a target. You've made your peace with both. Mayor of Kingstown belongs to people who understand that keeping the peace is not the same as being at peace — and who do the job regardless.

Four love stories, four completely different ideas of what it means to be somebody's person. This is the one that matches how you actually fall, and what it would ask of you.

You do not fall for strangers. You fall for the person you have already been talking to for years, which is the slowest and by some distance the most durable way anybody has found to do this.

You want a partner, not an audience. Somebody who wants their own life as badly as you want yours, and who would never ask you to be smaller to make the relationship easier.
