Three Ways to Master the Noir Aesthetic

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Dior’s American Psycho pieces bring a dark cinematic edge to modern menswear.

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Film noir has given menswear some of its most enduring images: the broad-shouldered suit, the long overcoat, the shirt with its collar open just enough to suggest trouble. The clothes are sharp, the colors run dark, and the men wearing them tend to look like they've got somewhere important to be. Even when they don't.

You don't need a private investigator's wardrobe to borrow from the genre. A well-cut suit will get you most of the way there, while a graphic T-shirt or a statement bag can bring the look into the present. Dior's American Psycho T-shirt and Book Tote offer a particularly good entry point, drawing on the cover art of Bret Easton Ellis's novel for a dose of dark literary and cinematic attitude. Here's how to make the aesthetic work in three different ways.

The suit is the natural starting point. Classic noir leads tend to wear clothes with presence: broad shoulders, defined waists, full-length trousers, and jackets with enough structure to hold their shape. You can take those proportions as inspiration without dressing like you've wandered off a 1940s soundstage.

Start with a charcoal or black suit, a white or pale blue shirt, and black leather shoes. A long wool overcoat adds another layer and gives the silhouette a little drama. Pay attention to fit, particularly through the shoulders and trouser leg. The goal is a clean line from top to bottom, not a suit so tight you can't sit through dinner.

For a more relaxed interpretation, replace the dress shirt with a fine-gauge black knit or wear the jacket with dark, straight-leg trousers. The result retains the polish of classic Hollywood while feeling appropriate for a modern wardrobe. Save the fedora for a particularly committed evening.

Film noir has always had a strong visual identity. A graphic T-shirt lets you reference that world without committing to a full suit. Dior's black American Psycho tee, which features embroidered artwork based on the first-edition cover of Bret Easton Ellis's notorious novel, brings a literary reference to a familiar menswear staple.

The trick is to keep the rest of the outfit simple. Wear the shirt with charcoal trousers, black jeans, or a pair of dark, pleated wool pants. Add a structured blazer if you want to sharpen things up, or wear it on its own with leather boots for a more casual look. Either way, let the graphic take center stage.

There's a good bit of irony in wearing American Psycho on your chest. Patrick Bateman, the novel's image-obsessed protagonist, treats clothes as a measure of status and a way to distinguish himself from everyone around him. The reference has some bite, particularly when paired with understated basics rather than an outfit that tries to do too much. You get a little cinematic attitude without looking like you're headed to a costume party.

A bag can change the character of an outfit, especially when the rest of the clothes are fairly restrained. The Large Book Tote features the same book-cover artwork as the matching tee. It has a practical shape, with room for a 15-inch laptop and everyday essentials, and can be carried by hand or over the shoulder.

The graphic gives the bag a personality of its own, so there's no need to build an entire outfit around it. Carry it with a long charcoal overcoat, a dark crewneck sweater, straight-leg trousers, and polished loafers. The combination nods to noir's shadowy palette while the bag adds a more contemporary point of interest. You don't need to dress like a private eye to make the reference land. A few well-chosen pieces will do the job.

Start with a dark suit, tailored trousers, a long overcoat, and polished leather shoes. For a more casual take, pair a graphic T-shirt with dark denim or pleated trousers. Keep the silhouette sharp and the palette restrained.

Mix classic tailoring with contemporary pieces. A fine-gauge knit can replace a dress shirt, while a graphic tee or statement bag adds personality to an otherwise understated outfit. The goal is to borrow from film noir's visual language without dressing for a period drama.

Black, charcoal, gray, navy, and white form the foundation of the look. High contrast, such as a pale shirt against a dark suit, is especially effective. You can also introduce muted brown or olive for a softer take that still feels true to the aesthetic.

Film noir style tends toward dark palettes, sharp tailoring, and an understated, slightly brooding mood. Old Hollywood style is a broader category that includes everything from formal tuxedos to relaxed studio-era tailoring. The two overlap, but film noir offers a more specific and shadowy approach to dressing.

A Guide to Cruelty-Free Skin Care

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

How to spot non-animal-tested formulas and the cleansers, serums, and moisturizers to start with.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Every skin care product should be cruelty-free, but in the world of beauty regulations, rules in the U.S. neither require brands to conduct animal testing to prove a product is safe nor prohibit the practice. Brands have proudly volunteered to be “cruelty-free” certified by organizations like Cruelty Free International (Leaping Bunny) and PETA, and a growing number of countries and states have banned or restricted animal testing. You can easily identify cruelty-free certified brands by their labeling (look for the bunny). And just because it’s not tested on animals doesn’t mean it’s any less effective or safe. To jump-start your not-animal-tested lineup or to introduce a new addition to your already existing range, here are some of the best cruelty-free skin care products.

This cream cleanser foams into a rich lather, gently clears away the day with the help of Japanese rice powder, and leaves behind hyaluronic acid and red algae so skin feels clean but still hydrated.

The Tri-ceramide complex in Byoma’s multitasking mask will help restore broken-down skin barriers and reinforce those already in good shape. The peel-off treatment reduces water loss, boosts collagen production, and includes calming cica to soothe redness and irritation.

If there’s a one-and-done moisturizer to add to your routine, make it this one. The lightweight Cloud Cushion cream is loaded with brightening and texture-smoothing niacinamide, complexion-evening peptides, multiple moisturizers (squalane and snow mushroom), and ceramides to keep skin from losing moisture.

For skin in need of advanced fine-line smoothing, a texture reboot, and serious free-radical-fighting, this potent antioxidant serum—which also has moisturizing hyaluronic acid and glycerin—is the answer.

A trio of proteins works in tandem with plant-derived exosomes and growth factors to smooth fine lines, improve elasticity, and boost collagen. If refining texture is the goal, this helps with that, too.

Retinol can be tricky with sensitive skin, but this firming moisturizer pairs the ingredient with ginseng ceramides that soothe any irritation, keep skin moisturized so the retinol doesn’t dry it out, and support the skin barrier. Copper peptides in the formula help skin look bouncier.

This fan-favorite sunscreen is also cruelty-free. The skin protector goes on completely clear, has a high SPF and antioxidants, and doubles as a makeup primer.

As the name suggests, Medicube’s Zero Pore Pads use a combination of alpha hydroxy acids and beta hydroxy acids to refine, unclog, and help pores look smaller (or nonexistent). The pads also have hyaluronic acid, soothing allantoin, and the anti-inflammatory panthenol—a combo that makes it a gentler choice for sensitive skin.

Part makeup, part eye treatment, DRMTLGY’s eye corrector is formulated in four tints for light coverage, has UV-blocking zinc oxide, and has moisturizing snow mushroom to keep the delicate under-eye area hydrated and plump, not puffy.

Another double-duty skin care-meets-makeup combo is this peptide-laced bronzing serum. Tiny capsules carrying the buildable tint are suspended in the serum, which is infused with smoothing bio-peptides and cacao extract to improve elasticity.

If you’re building a cruelty-free skin care regimen, don’t forget about the lips. Mango butter and argan oil are the hero ingredients in Versed’s lip mask, which delivers a smooth, moisturized, non-sticky finish.

Reformation’s Next Chapter Is Happening at Home

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Because good style doesn’t end in your closet.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Reformation fans have cause to rejoice today, as the beloved brand just announced its first-ever foray into home goods. This morning, Reformation launched its collaboration with French rugmaker Les Editions de Tapis.

Lauren Caris Cohan, chief creative officer at Reformation, has worked with Les Editions de Tapis for years, bringing to life rugs for Reformation stores. According to Cohan, customers have a habit of asking where the rugs are from. Now, they can bring them home for themselves.

The inspiration for the designs is rooted in French elegance and L.A. nonchalance, according to Cohan. “I think that combination is very Ref: something romantic and considered, with enough ease that it never feels precious,” she tells ELLE. “There’s a sense of humour, but it’s subtle. A rug can be a serious piece of craftsmanship and still have a cat walking across it. That balance feels very true to Reformation.”

The collection comprises nine made-to-order rugs, three of which are chintz, designed in collaboration between Cohan and Les Editions de Tapis founder Thomas Coccimiglio.

“Thomas and I have a longstanding creative partnership,” Cohan told ELLE. “Les Editions de Tapis has made exquisite rugs for our stores for nearly five years now, and we get such lovely feedback about those spaces. Bringing a special piece of that experience into our customers’ homes felt like a natural next step.”

Each rug is hand-knotted by artisans in India and Nepal. Wool, linen, and silk are featured throughout. The rugs are exclusively available online, and prices range from $1,000 to $5,000. Shop them at thereformation.com.

Castlebound devs explain a unique Unity technique behind its Paper Mario-like visuals

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Ogre Pixel director Steve Durán explains how the studio’s storybook co-op platformer gets its depth from Unity settings rather than 3D models.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

A decade ago, indie developer Ogre Pixel released a mobile game called Warcher Defenders, a tiny retro-style action RPG about defending a castle from waves of enemies. That has served as the inspiration behind the studio's latest game, Castlebound.

"For Castlebound, we took the enemy waves idea from Warcher Defenders and took it to the next level by allowing the heroes to move around the entire screen with auto-scrolling levels, while also making it a solo or co-op experience for up to four players," says Ogre Pixel director Steve Durán.

Another level-up is in the visuals. Whereas Warcher Defenders used pixel art, Castlebound takes a more hand-drawn illustrative approach reminiscent of a children's storybook. That said, the aesthetic still draws inspiration from Warcher Defenders, as the main character, Lenora, is an archer like the mobile game's first playable character. As a co-op game, it also offers other playable heroes designed around different gameplay styles, including Vargus the Mage (also Lenora's wise grandfather), Galahad the Knight, and Quinn the Slingshooter.

According to Durán, the decision behind this charming art style was that it was also "easy to read for the action gameplay. It does have design advantages because having the heroes represented with 2D sprites and a sticker-esque outline makes all the action to be easily identifiable."

It's also reminiscent of the paper cutouts of the characters in Paper Mario, which gives it license to use a limited set of animations rather than the painstakingly smooth, hand-drawn animations of Hollow Knight.

Similar to Paper Mario is how these obviously flat characters are presented in 3D world, or at least the illusion of one. "Something interesting about the Castlebound’s art style is that all the assets made by our artists are flat sprites exported as PNGs," Durán explains. "The 3D look comes from a configuration we have inside Unity, the game engine we used."

He credits the studio's lead programmer, Marco Elizondo, for using Unity to create 3D scenes that the sprites can be added to, as well as teaching the studio's artists how to create these scenarios with 2D assets and scene configurations.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

"Another important tool we worked with are illuminations settings to change environments between day, noon, and night," Durán adds. Sometimes, a lighting change is all you need to make a level feel different, while 3D effects like shadows also add depth.

There's also variety to the enemy designs, which gradually expand beyond the initial medieval fantasy tropes of wizards, dragons, and - true to the studio's name - ogres. Durán confirms that players can also expect to encounter enemies inspired by ancient Egyptian culture, such as mummies, pyramids and sphinxes, as well as from horror culture, such as vampires, werewolves, and mermen. "We also have a secret area that we don’t want to spoil, but it does have some kind of cosmic vibes," he teases.

During Castlebound's development, the Mexico-based studio has also been busy with other smaller projects, releasing titles on Steam during its two-year development, including the cosy hidden object game Hidden Around The World and the GBA-inspired adventure Black Tower Enigma this year alone.

"Castlebound has definitely been for us a bigger development endeavour because we have been iterating a lot of its development features like art, gameplay, and game balance," says Durán. "Moving into different projects has helped us feel 'fresh' during all this time."

Although still a relatively small game, with a roguelite loop that keeps each run feeling dynamic and different, a last-minute Kickstarter campaign has also enabled extra features, including a new volcano area, a secret final boss, and animated cutscenes. Together, they create a more substantial package that will help Castlebound stand out in a busy holiday release schedule.

Castlebound releases on 12 November for PC, Nintendo Switch 2, and Nintendo Switch, and you can download a free demo on Steam.

Work has appeared in print including Edge, Official Playstation Magazine, GamesMaster, Games TM, Wireframe, Stuff, and online including Kotaku UK, TechRadar, FANDOM, Rock Paper Shotgun, Digital Spy, The Guardian, and The Telegraph.

Final Fantasy 7 Revelation's new party members and job system make combat fresh again

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Between Summer Game Fest, Gamescom, and the PlayStation State of Play, director Naoki Hamaguchi and the team at Square Enix have showcased Final Fantasy 7 Revelation's massive open world. It's a huge sandbox brimming with activities, map markers, and interruptions from the slightly overtuned robotic companion MAI.

It's been an overwhelming amount of content packed into tight demos. However, after spending some time with the first chapter in a brand new preview session, I finally feel like I have a better handle on the huge offering in store. It’s clear that FF7 Revelation's scope tightens quite a bit as Cloud and the rest of the party make haste to the North Crater to stop Sephiroth.

Developer: In-housePublisher: Square EnixPlatform(s): PC, PS5, Xbox Series X, Nintendo Switch 2Release date: April 7, 2027

This roughly 45-minute linear dungeon presents a more grounded look at the narrative parts of FF7 Revelation as we see Cloud and the gang still reeling from the loss of Aerith. Now focused on avenging her at any cost, the party urgently presses forward through the twisting and turning paths of the North Crater as they hustle toward the center.

Cloud's mental state is still rapidly declining and Sephiroth's hold over him grows stronger. Traversing the dangerous caverns and roads is broken up by intermittent cutscenes of our silver-haired villain telling Cloud to remember his SOLDIER number. To remember where he came from. These small but powerful moments remind me what I love so much about the FF7 Remake trilogy: the mystery and intoxicating intrigue of Sephiroth, and how it envelops everything around it.

As I cut through enemies like the tentacle-ridden Vitalipolis on my way through this Mako-infused crater, I begin to experiment with the new Function-Integrated Tactical Suitwear (or FITS for short). This system is a smaller version of a traditional Job system found in other Final Fantasy games and gives each character a unique suite of abilities to complement their kits.

With the remake trilogy drawing to a close early 2027, we get ready for the epic conclusion with our Final Fantasy 7 Revelation Big Preview across hands-on impressions, dev access, and deep dives into the whole adventure so far.

Cloud is equipped with the Summoner FIT, which provides the ability to – you guessed it – summon classic creatures such as Shiva and Ifrit. Just like any other ability in Final Fantasy 7, you'll need ATB gauge to spend, and these new powers can be found under a special slot in the tactical menu (or bound to one of four shortcut keys). Having Shiva fight alongside the party and attack in sync with my button presses provides spectacle, but the fights aren't long enough to see real depth in the moment-to-moment decisions.

Cid Highwind finally takes up his lance and joins the party as a playable character alongside emo vampire heartthrob Vincent Valentine. Both additions immediately slot into the combat system with ease. Cid's flashy Dragoon attacks provide a sense of mobility, letting him easily jump and dash around the battlefield. There's more depth beneath the surface with Cid's combat kit, though. Timing your button presses with the blue aura flashes around him presents an opportunity for stronger attacks, adding another plate to spin during combat and one that rewards the player in spades with long sweeping swipes and a whirlwind of pain.

Weekly digests, tales from the communities you love, and more

Vincent Valentine's brooding looks are accompanied by deadly accurate pistols. He twirls around the arena dishing out ranged attacks and applying pressure before spending ATB bar to transform into the Galian Beast, Vincent's true bestial nature unleashed. But, this does come at a cost. Chaos Levels build up the longer I stay in beast form, with each new level reducing my max HP. So far, this hasn't kicked me out of Galian Beast form, but it's clear that this is another resource to be managed in the middle of hectic battles.

Both Cid and Vincent have deceptively simple combo strings. At first it seems like a straightforward "mash square and spam abilities until the enemy is dead," but more layers continually unfold. Playing these characters feels more akin to a rhythm game, precisely timing button presses to match the combat pace. I still know there's more to how they fit into the larger team composition, but a short demo was never going to allow for understanding the full breadth of how it all fits together.

Eventually, I find myself going toe to toe with another version of the extraterrestrial lifeform Jenova, a rather straightforward fight that largely consists of whaling on a stationary target and staggering it for extra damage. Large undulating tentacles glommed onto the outskirts of the arena. I'm quickly reminded of the sheer spectacle that the Remake series always puts forth, and it's clear that Final Fantasy 7 Revelation continues to harbor that mantra.

At the end of the combat section there's one final tease of something greater. Sephiroth appears and takes the crew to the events of Nibelheim that started it all. Cloud and the gang are confronted with that horrid day as Sephiroth walks through a dark portal begging to be followed.

The party sees a frozen Zack Fair, questioning his relationship to it all. My own head swirling with questions alongside them. Just as Cloud walks through, the demo ends. I don't know what's on the other side of that portal, but this small slice of Revelation puts its characters and story first.

There's still so much to see as the final chapter of the Final Fantasy 7 remake trilogy hurdles toward its conclusion, and this window into it all has me excited to see what's on the other side of it all.

Far from final, you can check out our best RPGs, best JRPGs, and best ARPGs rankings for what to play next, and keep an eye on our upcoming RPGs calendar for more!

You can find him playing the critically acclaimed MMORPG Final Fantasy XIV or whatever hot new multiplayer game his friends are playing.

Manchester United must buy Mauricio Pochettino out of his United States contract, even if it comes at an uncomfortable cost

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

If there is ever a moment to enact a mid-season managerial change, the November international break is it.

Manchester United do not kick a ball again until November 22, presenting the Old Trafford hierarchy with an uninterrupted fortnight to recalibrate, install fresh ideas, and arrest what has been an alarmingly concerning start to the season.

But, by all accounts, they're not going to take the opportunity.

On the pitch, the case for change has long ceased to be a debate. A return of just one win from ten Premier League matches under Michael Carrick is relegation form and the bottom three is where Man United find themselves.

The team looks passive, confidence is shot, and the trajectory is pointing downwards, even in the face of two improbable Old Trafford comebacks in the space of a week. Despite having a long-admired, elite manager reportedly receptive to an approach, Man United appear set to soldier on with Carrick. The reason, as is so often the case in modern football, may very well come down to money.

According to talkSPORT, Mauricio Pochettino remains open to the prospect of taking over at Old Trafford. The former Tottenham and Chelsea boss has been near the top of Man United’s wish list for nearly a decade, repeatedly identified as an ideal fit throughout the club's post-Sir Alex Ferguson malaise.

The obstacle, however, lies in the United States as Pochettino is contracted to the United States Men’s National Team (USMNT), having recently committed to an extension following their 2026 home World Cup.

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

Poaching him would require Man United to buy him out of that deal. In 2025, reports claimed that Pochettino’s USMNT contract contained a £20m break clause, a valuation corroborated by ESPN’s reporting that any release fee would sit comfortably in the "tens of millions."

Compounding that outlay is the compensation package that would be due to Carrick and his staff if the club chose to make a change. Less than 12 months ago, Manchester United dismissed Ruben Amorim, whose severance package cost the club an estimated £10m.

Under the scrutiny of the Premier League's Squad Cost Ratio (SCR) rules, and with INEOS tasked with trimming the operational budget, Man United’s hesitation is understandable. However, dressing up Carrick’s stay as an executive structure exercising patience with a long-term project is disingenuous. Sticking with the current coaching setup could easily be seen as a cost-saving exercise.

The danger is that short-term financial caution risks baking in long-term sporting problems. Ducking a payout today protects the club's bottom line, but missing out on European qualification or sliding into a genuine relegation dogfight will end up costing tenfold in lost prize money, reduced commercial clout and squad devaluation.

Pochettino has the authority and Premier League nous to reset Man United, and the November calendar gives them the cleanest possible runway to embed him, at least until the next international break in March, but that is too long into the season to salvage an ailing campaign.

Letting this window pass without making a change in the dugout has the potential to define Manchester United's 2026/27.

Joe joined FourFourTwo as senior digital writer in July 2025 after five years covering Leeds United in the Championship and Premier League. The future of flight meets space exploration as a futuristic electric aircraft hovers beside Blue Origin's massive New Glenn rocket stage.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

A futuristic single-person aircraft takes to the skies at NASA's Kennedy Space Center in Florida, with the upper stage of Blue Origin's New Glenn rocket providing a dramatic backdrop.

The unusual scene was captured during the MAX POWER event, which brought together innovators and emerging technologies at the iconic spaceflight facility.

The photograph shows a single-person electric aircraft called Jetson ONE, developed by Jetson, a Swedish startup company working to bring personal flight to the skies.

The compact rotorcraft uses multiple propellers to climb into the air, allowing it to take off and vertically without the need for a runway.

The aircraft was performing flight demonstrations during the UP.Summit gathering, which formed part of the larger MAX POWER event at the Kennedy Space Center.

During the demonstrations, the Jetson ONE demonstrated its ability to maneuver through the air and hover in place, giving spectators a close-up look at the technology in action.

The photograph offers a fascinating glimpse at two very different approaches to flight. While Jetson is developing small electric aircraft designed for personal transportation, Blue Origin's New Glenn rocket is built to carry payloads far beyond Earth's atmosphere.

Daisy Dobrijevic joined Space.com in February 2022, having previously worked as a staff writer for All About Space magazine. She completed an editorial internship with BBC Sky at Night Magazine and worked at the National Space Centre, communicating space science to the public.

Daisy holds a PhD in plant physiology and a Master's in Environmental Science. Based in Nottingham, U.K., she covers all things space, with a special focus on solar activity and space weather. Baby chimpanzees blow ‘raspberries’ because adults do it first

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 9, 2026 3:00 PM EST

The old adage “monkey see, monkey do,” appears to have a bit of truth to it. Chimpanzees (Pan troglodytes) likely watch adults making different vocalization to learn how and when to use those sounds themselves, particularly when they purse their lips to blow “raspberries.” The findings detailed in a study published today in the journal Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences (PNAS) show that the primates’ sounds are not instinctual and set in stone, as previously believed.

Humans learn what words mean from the world around us, and different communities will use different sounds for the same thing. For example, English speakers mimic dogs with “woof,” while French speakers say “ouaf.” When it comes to our primate relatives, biologists generally used to believe that they didn’t learn sounds from one another, and that instead biology dictated their sounds. However, that’s changing with some new research into two groups of Chimpanzees in a couple of west African countries.

To probe how chimpanzees learn to use sounds, the study’s team focused on the raspberry. Chimpanzees make this unvoiced sound that’s similar to how humans push their lips together to blow raspberries. The team looked at how 91 chimpanzees, from birth to 55 years old, used the raspberry call. They studied a group of wild chimpanzees from four communities in Taï National Park, Ivory Coast, and a group of young, orphaned, and peer-raised chimpanzees at Tacugama Chimpanzee Sanctuary in Sierra Leone.

The team found that the wild Ivory Coast chimpanzees almost exclusively used the raspberry while grooming others. However, the juveniles at the Tacugama Chimpanzee Sanctuary used it across a much wider range of social situations.

If blowing a raspberry is only an instinctive call, the groups should be using it in similar ways no matter where they were raised. According to the team, these differences suggest that chimpanzees learn from adults how to make a call, as well as when and why to use it.

“In everyday communication, we use the words that we do only because our peers do too. It’s communal practice that fixes their meaning, rather than features of the words themselves,” Richard Moore, a study co-author and mind, language, and psychology philosopher at the University of Warwick, said in a statement.

“The discovery that this is true not just for natural languages but also some chimpanzee calls means there’s more continuity between chimpanzees’ vocal learning and our own than we previously suspected.”

The team also say that when an adult chimpanzee was introduced to one of the groups living in the Tacugama sanctuary, the young chimps gradually began to use the raspberry more consistently and began to resemble the adult’s noises. This suggests that adults serve as role models, helping a community settle in on shared communication patterns.

The researchers say this is the first time that this kind of learned and agreed-upon sound use has been shown in chimpanzee calls. The link between the raspberry sound and its use appears to be shared from one chimpanzee to another, and is not built in at birth. While chimpanzees struggle to learn entirely new call sounds, the study suggests that they can learn the meaning behind sounds that they already know how to make but didn’t use in the same way as other chimps.

“Even aspects of chimpanzee vocal communication that seemed fixed and unlearned may be shaped by learning, suggesting that there is still much to discover about their vocal repertoire,”

added Antoine Valet, a study co-author and cognitive scientist at the Institute of Cognitive Sciences (CNRS) in France.

Additionally, the team has some theories on why different chimpanzee populations use the raspberry in similar ways if it is a learned sound.

“One possibility is that, whilst grooming, as chimpanzees use their lips to extract parasites from another’s fur, it may be easier to generate a vocalisation that uses similar lip movements, a call that is known to prolong grooming bouts,” explained study co-author and CNRS behavioral ecologist Catherine Crockford. “Communities may have found this useful independently, but that wouldn’t change the fact that the call is a convention. We need more research to test it.”

Report Puts AI At 76B Monthly Sessions, Drops Search Engine Comparison via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Calls from ChatGPT and Claude often get filed as direct. This checklist shows how to track AI search as its own call source.

Calls from ChatGPT and Claude often get filed as direct. This checklist shows how to track AI search as its own call source.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Guide: AI Max Drove 68% Of Retail Invalid Clicks

An updated AI usage report says it can no longer reliably compare AI used for search with search engines. AI referrals grew but stay a small share of traffic.

Graphite’s updated AI usage report puts six AI assistants at 76 billion sessions a month worldwide in September, up 37% from a year earlier. Unlike the agency’s March edition, it doesn’t compare that usage with search engines.

In March, the agency put monthly AI sessions at 56% the size of search worldwide and 34% in the U.S., with search-related AI use at 28% and 17%. The new report says that edition likely undercounted Google search use from Google’s Android app, and that the agency “can no longer reliably compare” the size of AI used for search with search engines.

The session counts are Similarweb estimates of web visits plus mobile app sessions. The report doesn’t include desktop apps because, as it explains, there isn’t reliable data available for them. Graphite sells SEO and AEO services, while Similarweb sells the data.

According to the March report, search engine usage was calculated based on website visits only (not including the apps), since the apps saw little use.

However, according to the November report, the Google Android app is different, accounting for an estimated 11.8 billion monthly sessions in the U.S. People use the app for image recognition, translation and Google Discover as well as general search. The agency says it has “no reliable data source” to determine what percent of these sessions are related to general search.

The March report’s 56% figure for total AI session usage used a web-based only count of search engines. In addition, the March report’s estimate that ChatGPT accounted for 20% of global search-related traffic used the same count. Although the new report does not revisit either of these numbers, as of this writing, the March page is still highlighting these numbers as some of the major findings from that report, though there is a link to the new report at the top.

The report’s data “suggest that AI accounts for less than 1% of traffic on average” to websites. A separate chart of U.S. traffic in the second quarter of 2026 puts organic search at 49 times the volume of AI referrals and paid search at eight times.

AI referral traffic has grown from that small base, up 92% year over year worldwide as of July and 75% in the U.S. The report’s referral figures cover the top 43,000 sites among domains with at least 100,000 monthly U.S. visits in six or more months between July 2023 and July 2026. It tracks AI referrals to those sites worldwide and in the U.S.

In July, ChatGPT accounted for 60% of AI referral traffic worldwide, followed by Gemini at 14% and Claude at 10%. In the U.S., ChatGPT’s share was 66%, while Claude accounted for 11% and Gemini for 10%.

Between July and December 2025, global session activity with the six assistants increased by 2.3%. The report describes this as a plateau. Then global activity with all the assistants rose by 19% from January through September 2026.

Year-over-year growth in U.S. session activity was 45% in September.

Worldwide session activity with ChatGPT grew 24% year over year. ChatGPT also represented 58.9% of the sessions added.

Claude had the largest increase (in percent) globally, at 1,901%. Claude also generated 26% of the additional sessions. Perplexity was the only assistant among the six to show a decline, falling 27% globally.

Most global sessions occurred on mobile (mobile app or mobile web), at 89% in the third quarter of 2026. Mobile comprised 78% of sessions in the United States.

Graphite’s data for Google in March was only from measuring the number of visits to Google’s website. The agency now says it can’t determine what percentage of the total usage of the Android app is search. OpenAI didn’t include the actual Google figure when it said that ChatGPT receives approximately one-fifth as many search-like queries as Google. I wrote about this on Nov. 3. The two estimates also count different things, with OpenAI logging a search whenever a question sends ChatGPT to the web and Graphite counting website visits and app sessions.

Before an AI-to-search ratio goes into a channel plan, check how its source counted Google.

Graphite cites anecdotal evidence that some visits prompted by AI answers get credited to direct visits or branded search, which AI referral reports wouldn’t catch. Its early numbers suggest branded search traffic is fairly flat year over year, with full results to come.

That measure is broader than the one in Similarweb’s June data, which I covered. Among people who visited a brand’s site after ChatGPT recommended it, 55.9% of that traffic came through branded search, in U.S. desktop data.

Ogilvy and CAA launch partnership to clone celebrity talent for AI ads

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

Body doubles are a popular subject in horror cinema. Usually, the doppelganger is the villain of the piece. But now, one of the leading gatekeepers in Hollywood is set to begin working with creative agency Ogilvy to run brand campaigns featuring entirely AI-generated celebrity appearances.

The WPP agency has inked a partnership with Creative Artists Agency (CAA) to generate AI ads featuring the actors, athletes and high-profile creators on CAA’s roster.

“We are witnessing the ‘NIL moment’ between advertising and entertainment,” said Carol Reed, global chief innovation officer at Ogilvy, referencing the Name, Image and Likeness industry that has grown around college sports influencers in the U.S. in recent years. “We’ve created a system where the biggest stars can license verified digital replicas safely… unlocking unprecedented agility for brands and assured control for talent.”

Brooklyn Nine-Nine star Terry Crews is among the actors who have opted in. Reed declined to name clients using the system, but said several campaigns were already being planned. “We are in the process of negotiating a few pilots right now that should be going live towards the end of the year,” she said.

The solution, called Echo, gives Ogilvy access to material kept in “The Vault” an in-house digital scanning facility built for CAA by tech company Veritone four years ago. Ogilvy and CAA’s partnership was a year in the making, per Reed. The studio uses 204 cameras to capture every facet of an actor, athlete or creator — from their voice to body language, facial expressions and the way they walk.

That material, which is refreshed by CAA clients every 3-5 years, is already used for de-aging or post-production fixes in films. Now, the agency’s staffers can pull it into WPP Open, the system behind the holding company’s various endeavors in creative production, agentic media buying and self-service creative tools, for internal storyboarding, mock-ups or final creative assets.

While the negotiations between actors or creators, their representatives and Ogilvy’s clients will still play out the same as always, the partnership is expected to yield more assets than classic shoots, at a faster pasce, and reducing cost-per-asset by 65-75%.

The combination of digital capture and AI content generation could “fundamentally change celebrity endorsement economics,” according to Gartner analyst Nicole Greene.

David Raichman, executive creative director and global AI creative lead at Ogilvy, called the system “the next frontier of content… more of it, faster, and – for the first time – more trusted, not less.”

If that sounds outlandish, consider the campaigns launched by Coca-Cola and Snickers earlier this year featuring soccer coach and CAA client José Mourinho — or rather, his digital clone. Ogilvy’s partnership with the agency, which Reed said could stretch to include other talent companies in the future, gives it the chance to scale up such projects.

Echo isn’t Ogilvy’s first effort in this realm. Five years ago, Ogilvy used digital scans of Bollywood star Shah Rukh Khan to advertise small businesses across India during the Hindu festival of Diwali. That project served as a kind of “prototype” for Echo, according to Reed.

She said the agency expects the partnership to be particularly useful for ads featuring sports personalities. “They’re locked into training and seasons, so there’s long periods where they’re not available,” she said. “Now they can kind of engage and work throughout a season where normally they wouldn’t be available.”

AI tools have become increasingly common in agency and in-house creative production efforts, though they’re typically deployed with performance media assets in mind — ads for digital display, paid social or mobile app environments — rather than campaign materials featuring a famous face. Though Reed said the agency expects to use the system initially for social assets, Ogilvy’s partnership gives it an edge for clients looking to take advantage of AI gains across the entire spectrum of advertising production.

“Agencies need a first-mover advantage right now,” said Greene.

For CAA, it’s a chance to “grow the pie,” according to Alex Shannon, the talent agency’s head of strategic development. Stars can take on commercial work unfettered by the schedule of their day job on the pitch or in the movie studio — allowing them to work with more brands overall, but also capitalize on moments when their profile might be highest, such as the climax to a sporting season or during the launch of a film or TV show.

“Now, brands have the ability to move at the speed of culture, and athletes are able to do even more to engage fans at times when that relevancy is so critically important,” she said.

The partnership between Ogilvy and CAA includes a framework designed to govern how and when the agency’s clients can use the likeness of talent. CAA clients own their digital likenesses, and are able to pick and choose which businesses and markets they’re happy to appear in ads for or formally endorse.

“Talent always gets to see everything and approve everything at the end,” said Reed. AI advertising materials will also include a watermark that clearly marks a video or image as both AI-generated and approved by the star in question. Reed said the measure is a means of reassuring consumers that “this isn’t a deep fake”, as well as staying ahead of future AI regulation. (The European Union, for example, has mandated gen AI providers put watermarks on content produced using their tools since August.)

CAA has been aggressive in defending the value of its clients’ likenesses, signing an agreement with YouTube to remove celeb deep fakes from the video platform in 2024. But while watermarks might keep Ogilvy clear of future AI rules, they might not aid consumer comprehension.

“The whole point of talent is to scale authentic relationships and credibility,” said Greene. Yet consumers, when asked, are typically cool on the idea of AI-generated glitterati hawking brands. Nearly half (49%) of Britons have a negative view of AI-generated actors appearing in ads, according to YouGov, while 92% said AI generated content makes it harder to trust information online in general.

Reed argued the system is principally an extension of Ogilvy’s “palette”, rather than a means of flooding the web with celebrity slop. “The intention is that still there is a human craft and a human behind it,” she said, adding that the agency still expects to recommend a traditional shoot when a situation demands it. “Authenticity is one of those things that is going to be even more important,” she said.

Reed said that the partnership could mean brands find it easier to work with several lower-profile names (and skip the usual scheduling difficulty) rather than shelling out for a single movie star. “I’m hoping that you’ll see more [brands] opening up to B-tier athletes,” she said.

That use case may prove particularly important to Ogilvy. CMOs place an increasingly high importance on their agencies’ ability to manage their large rosters of creators and influencers, and to do so in a cost-effective manner. Eliminating the need for a TikTok personality or podcaster to actually be on location could add a sharp edge to the agency’s current pitch.

Advertisers now demand creator metrics like cost per lead and conversions, not follower counts.

The AI platform is testing an analytics dashboard that counts how often a business shows up in ChatGPT’s answers.

Despite leaning on creators for most of the year, marketers aren’t doing it for the holiday crunch. That job still belongs to celebrities.

Polymarket Expands Chainlink-Powered 15-Minute Markets to Gold and Silver

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Polymarket and Chainlink announced an expansion of their oracle partnership on October 29, bringing high-speed up/down prediction markets to gold and silver and extending the concept beyond crypto assets. The announcement says Chainlink has supported more than $11 billion in trading volume on the platform, but that figure is not a forecast of what the new contracts will attract.

Prediction markets need more than a collection of traders. They also need an agreed answer to the question being traded: what happened to the underlying price by the end of the contract? The shorter the contract, the more carefully that answer needs to be defined.

That is the backdrop to a Chainlink announcement on October 29. It says Polymarket is deepening its use of Chainlink as an official oracle for rapid up/down markets, including new exposure to gold and silver. The companies describe an expansion of 15-minute formats into commodities, equities and foreign-exchange markets.

An up/down contract asks whether a reference price ends above or below a specified level at a particular time. That sounds straightforward, but two market-data sources can disagree briefly, especially when instruments trade around the clock or a contract ends during a volatile period.

The oracle determines which information enters the settlement process. For a high-frequency market, the timing convention, reference venue, publication frequency and dispute-handling rules can be as important as the price direction itself.

Chainlink says its infrastructure has already supported more than $11 billion in Polymarket trading volume. That is presented as activity processed through the partnership to date, not the size of the new gold and silver market or evidence of profit generated by participants.

For traders, a well-designed oracle should reduce ambiguity about the outcome. It cannot remove all market risk, guarantee execution at a desired price or ensure every contract is suitable for every jurisdiction. Prediction market access and the legality of particular contracts also vary across regions.

Moving from cryptocurrency prices to precious metals introduces differences in trading hours, underlying reference instruments and price discovery. Spot metals, futures and other benchmarks can move together without matching tick for tick. A contract’s methodology must therefore explain which index or feed is authoritative.

The same challenge becomes more complicated with foreign exchange and equities. Equities may not trade continuously in the same way as crypto, while different FX reference points could apply across market sessions. Speed alone is not a substitute for clearly documented market rules.

The announcement is also notable because it shows crypto infrastructure being repurposed for financial questions outside the digital-asset market. NewsBTC has covered planned infrastructure for tokenized securities, a separate example of blockchain providers competing on market infrastructure rather than token speculation alone.

The next useful figures would be contract-level trading volume, order-book depth, dispute frequency and the ability of markets to settle accurately during difficult price moves. These operational measures are more informative than the existence of a new trading pair.

There are also consumer-protection questions. Fifteen-minute contracts can encourage frequent decisions, and transaction costs or spreads can materially affect outcomes. They should not be presented as straightforward substitutes for holding gold, silver or an exchange-traded investment.

Polymarket and Chainlink have announced an extension of their data partnership into new underlying markets. The significance will depend on whether the contracts attract sustained use and whether the oracle and settlement rules perform reliably when prices move quickly.

Prediction market broker River Markets registers affiliate as CFTC futures commission merchant

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

River Markets was founded in May and has routed over 250 million contracts. It's backed by "top-tier venture capital firms" following a $8.5 million seed funding round.

River Markets said its affiliate, River Finance LLC, has registered with the Commodity Futures Trading Commission as a futures commission merchant (FCM) and joined the National Futures Association (NFA), allowing it to carry customer accounts and handle client funds.

The company is building a prime brokerage platform for prediction markets that aims to let institutional traders manage capital, positions, and risk across multiple exchanges and clearinghouses through a single account, with plans to support financing arrangements and permitted over-the-counter trading.

"Our conversations with the institutions entering this market give us a unique view of what they need," Cofounder and CEO Oscar Levy said in a release. "Access to event contracts isn’t enough. They need to use capital efficiently and manage risk around the clock, across exchanges. That’s the brokerage model we’re building."

River Markets was founded in May and serves hedge funds, sports funds, proprietary trading firms, and event-risk hedging companies, according to the firm. River Markets has routed over 250 million contracts and is backed by "top-tier venture capital firms" following its $8.5 million seed funding round.

Combined trading volume across Kalshi, Polymarket US, and Polymarket's international platform exceeded $500 billion in October. Prediction markets are becoming large enough to create demand for the kinds of brokerage, clearing, financing, and risk-management services traditionally associated with derivatives markets.

In July, Polymarket-affiliated entities sought an FCM and swap-firm registration through the NFA to enable U.S. customers to trade using borrowed capital. Kalshi affiliate Kinetic Markets received NFA approval as an FCM and swap firm in March. And last month, Blockchain.com applied for both a designated contract market (DCM) license and FCM registration.

Of note, Hyperliquid Strategies last week filed to become an FCM to carry U.S. client accounts for HIP-3 perpetual futures markets, an effort to help bring permissioned Hyperliquid markets to U.S. customers within a regulated framework.

Nevertheless, I still think it’s bizarre that a wholly owned Sony entity continues to release games not available on its parent company’s platform.

Before Euphoria, Sydney Sweeney Starred In An Overlooked Psychological Thriller That's Streaming For Free

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

2019 was a big year for Sydney Sweeney, marking her debut as Cassie Howard in HBO's "Euphoria." That proved to be her breakout role, but it wasn't her only memorable performance of 2019. That same year appeared in "Clementine" as Lana, a mysterious and beguiling figure who enters into a complicated relationship with another woman trying to reckon with a recently-ended love affair. Like Sweeney's character, the movie is hard to pin down. As Variety's Lisa Kennedy noted in her review, you're never quite sure whether this is "a coming-of-age meditation or a psychosexual thriller," a "breakup drama or simmering horror flick," making it a compelling watch throughout. All of which makes it a shame this one got lost in the shuffle — a result of having been overshadowed by Sweeney's "Euphoria" debut and the global pandemic of 2020. Thankfully, "Clementine" is now available to watch for free over on one of the best streaming services available, Tubi.

Sweeney hasn't always had the easiest time outside of "Euphoria." Thanks to the $100 million box office smash hit "Anyone But You" and her lead performance in Paul Feig's excellent, wicked drama "The Housemaid," the actor has managed to remain relevant. But Sweeney's 2025 film "Americana" was ignored at the box office and "Madame Web" speaks for itself.

That said, Sweeney has always subverted expectations and will no doubt continue to see success in the coming years. Heck, she has already overcome perceptions of her as an attractive young actor with little else to offer via excellent performances in a range of projects, many of which simply haven't been given the attention they deserve. "Clementine" is just one example.

/Film's list of the best Sydney Sweeney movies is a testament to the actor's versatility, with everything from romantic comedies to the intense, nerve wracking portrait of United States Air Force veteran Reality Winner that was "Reality." 2019's "Clementine" is harder to define but just as worthy of your attention.

The film is written and directed by Lara Jean Gallagher, and represents the Pennsylvania-native's debut narrative feature following a series of short films and commercials. It tells the story of 30-year-old Karen (Otmara Marrero), who, having broken up with her older girlfriend, D (Sonya Walger), travels to her ex's lake house to take stock of the relationship. There, she meets 19-year-old Lana (Sweeney), who's first seen sunbathing by the lake and very quickly captures Karen's attention. As the pair grow closer, Karen suddenly finds herself in a similar situation to D in her former relationship. Wary of developing feelings for someone significantly younger, yet hopelessly drawn to Lana, Karen has to navigate a delicate relationship.

Gallagher told Movablefest that with "Clementine" she wanted to interrogate the often contradictory nature of female relationships. "I was just really just interested in exploring female relationships," she said, "and how it can feel like a sister dynamic or like rivals, or like a mother/daughter at times or even sexual in nature all at the same time." That can be seen in the often confounding connection between Karen and Lana, which  makes for a movie that's at once tense and touching, hazy yet sharp.

Prior to her "Euphoria" debut, Sydney Sweeney was making a name for herself with roles in Netflix series "Everything Sucks!" and HBO's "Sharp Objects," in which she played a psychiatric patient Alice alongside Amy Adams' Camille Preaker. Sweeney also played Eden Spencer in Season 2 of "The Handmaid's Tale" and her star was clearly on the rise. It's a shame, then, that "Clementine" flew under the radar in the way that it did, as it boasts an enigmatic yet darkly enchanting performance from Sweeney that surely would have helped boost her profile had the film received more attention.

"Clementine" premiered at the 2019 Tribeca Film Festival ahead of distributor Oscilloscope Pictures releasing the film on virtual screens in May 2020 — a late pivot that came in the wake of the global pandemic. It managed a perfectly respectable 62% critic score on Rotten Tomatoes, and while that may seem slightly underwhelming, certain critics were all in on Lara Gallagher's psychological thriller.

The aforementioned Lisa Kennedy praised Sweeney for "deftly shape-shift[ing] from late teen to younger — too young? — often within the same scene," while Slant's Jake Cole was convinced that Gallagher is "every bit as adept as Josephine Decker and the Safdie brothers are at wringing unease from the most mundane of scenarios." David Ehrlich of IndieWire might have felt the film was "too prone to navel-gazing to leave a strong impression" but Nell Minow of RogerEbert.com seemed to enjoy what she described as an "exploration of the shifting power in relationships with striking images and overtones of duality." So, if you're looking for a good psychological thriller, "Clementine" is arguably one of the best movies on Tubi right now.

Claes Bang Heinrich Himmler Masseur Drama ‘I Is Another’ Sells Wide For Beta Cinema – AFM

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Felix Randau’s post-World War Two drama I Is Another, starring Claes Bang as Heinrich Himmler’s real-life controversial massage therapist Felix Kersten, has sold to a raft of territories in the wake of its Locarno Piazza Grande world premiere over the summer.

Sales agent Beta Cinema, which will be screening the film at the AFM, is reporting all rights deals to France (ARP Selection), Spain (Youplanet Pictures), Scandinavia (Angel Films), Hungary (Vertigo Media), Bulgaria (Beta Film), Israel (Red Cape), India ), India (Big Tree Entertainment) and to Australia & New Zealand (Palace Films).

Berlin-based Port-au-Prince Pictures (The Secret Agent, System Crasher) is set to release the film in Germany in early 2027. Watch on Deadline

ARP Selection, the upcoming slate of which also includes Sundance breakout Josephine and Belgian World War Two resistance drama Le Faux Soir, is currently introducing the I Is Another to French audiences at the Augenblick Film Festival, ahead of a theatrical release in 2027.

Randau (Iceman), who also wrote the screenplay, spent years researching the extraordinary story of Kersten, who claimed to have used his influence over SS founder and Holocaust architect Himmler to save thousands of people caught in the crosshairs of the Nazi regime.

Bang is joined in the cast by Valerie Pachner (A Hidden Life) in the role of investigative journalist Hedda, who ambushes Kersten on the night train to Oslo as he travels with his wife to the Norwegian Nobel Institute in the expectation that he will receive its coveted peace prize.

Hedda, who is hiding secrets of her own, has doubts about Kersten’s accounts of his conduct during World War Two, and is determined to get to the bottom of the truth. A night long psychological game of cat and mouse ensues.

I Is Another is produced by ostlicht filmproduktion (The Auschwitz Report) in co-production with Veit Heiduschkas Wega Film (The White Ribbon).

Sebastian Thaler (Transatlantic) serves as director of photography. The cast also includes Susanne Wuest (Sound of Falling), Timocin Ziegler (Learning to Die) and Martin Müller (The Universal Theory).

EXCLUSIVE: Coming off his first two sold-out arena headlining shows and the No. 1 U.S. debut of his first stand-up special, Netflix’s I Know You Lying, comedian Dyon “Mojo” Brooks has added 25 dates to his 2027 Outta Pocket Tour.

This includes arena headlining performances at Houston’s Toyota Center on March 27, Atlanta’s State Farm Arena on April 16 and St. Louis’ Chaifetz Arena on June 4.

The artist presale begins Wednesday, Nov. 11 at 10 a.m. local time. All presales conclude Wednesday, Nov. 11 at 11:59 p.m. local time, with the general on-sale beginning Thursday, Nov. 12 at 10 a.m. local time. View Brooks’ dates below. The newly announced shows extend Brooks’ biggest headlining tour to date, with additional stops in Providence, Raleigh, Washington, D.C., San Antonio, Cincinnati and more. His expanded routing includes a previously announced April 17 appearance at Nashville’s Bridgestone Arena. Watch on Deadline

A comedian, actor, writer and producer (also known as Mojo Brookzz) with millions of followers across social, Brooks has had an astonishingly rapid ascent from viral digital creator to sold-out arena headliner — he’s gone from headlining clubs to theaters to arenas in less than two years’ time.

Brooks’ recent pair of sold-out arena shows took place at Wintrust Arena his hometown of Chicago. Outside of stand-up, Brooks has been seen in Tyler Perry’s Netflix series Miss Governor and Nick Cannon’s Wild ‘n Out. He is repped by Bernie Cahill and Grant Pennel at Activist Artists Management, WME and Todd Rubenstein at Yorn Levin, Barnes.

Watch: Cisse and Pulisic stay red-hot in Milan’s battling win over Genoa – the highlights

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

AC Milan needed a win before the second international break of the season and they got it, beating Genoa 2-1 away from home.

It was never going to be an easy game against a side that had won three in a row and four of their last five, especially after Thursday’s defeat to Ferencvaros in the Europa League added a lot of pressure on Milan to put things right.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

The Rossoneri silenced a hostile home crowd who seemed well up for it with two goals inside the opening 20 minutes. Alphadjo Cisse got the first – his eighth of the season – from Adrien Rabiot’s feed, then Christian Pulisic buried Goncalo Ramos’ square ball to put Ruben Amorim’s side in command.

Inside the final 20 minutes or so Milan dropped off a bit and Genoa had a late surge in hopes of snatching a point. Ellertsson’s strike from range halved the deficit, but the Diavolo held on to move back into the top four with a hard-fought win.

Premier League clubs are reportedly keeping a close watch on Lens defender Ismaelo Ganiou, who is attracting significant interest following his impressive emergence in French football.

According to an exclusive report from TEAMtalk, Tottenham Hotspur, Newcastle United and Chelsea are among the clubs monitoring the highly rated youngster, who has drawn comparisons with former Real Madrid defender Raphael Varane.

The 20-year-old centre-back has made considerable progress over the past year, with several European heavyweights now assessing his potential.

Tottenham and Newcastle have both been following Ganiou’s development, while Chelsea’s ownership group, BlueCo, are also understood to be monitoring his situation. Fulham and Brentford have joined the list of English clubs keeping tabs on the defender.

Manchester City, Paris Saint-Germain, Borussia Dortmund and RB Leipzig are also aware of his potential, highlighting the growing competition for his signature.

The Lens youngster could be an exciting long-term addition for clubs looking to strengthen their defensive options. His pace, physical presence and composure on the ball make him an intriguing prospect.

Ganiou has already earned recognition for his performances at senior level, and Lens demonstrated their confidence in him by handing him a new four-year contract during the summer.

The comparisons with Varane are particularly interesting given that both defenders developed through Lens’ academy system.

Like the former France international, Ganiou possesses the athleticism and defensive qualities needed to establish himself at the highest level. His development has also attracted attention from the French national setup, with the youngster earning opportunities at Under-21 level after previously representing Burkina Faso in friendly matches.

His potential is also gaining recognition in France, with Zinedine Zidane reportedly considering him as a possible option for the senior national team.

It remains to be seen whether any of his admirers will make a concrete move, but Ganiou’s rapidly increasing reputation suggests that competition for his services could intensify.

2027 Grammy Nominations Final Predictions: Album of the Year

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Both Olivias – Dean and Rodrigo – are likely to make it. Who will join them?

Ella Langley’s dream year is likely to culminate in a sweep when the nominations for the 69th annual Grammy Awards are announced on Monday (Nov. 16).

Thanks to a rule change in the best new artist category that was announced in June, which allows an artist to be entered four times instead of three, Langley could be nominated in four of the six general field categories — album, record and song of the year plus best new artist. If that happens, Langley will become the first country crossover artist to achieve the feat since Bobbie Gentry in 1968.

We first previewed the Grammy nominations in these marquee categories back in July. Starting today, we’re going to update those predictions, as needed. First up, album of the year, probably the most prestigious and coveted of the 100 Grammy Awards. On Tuesday, we’ll look at record and song of the year. On Wednesday, we’ll take on best new artist.

For the fourth year in a row, there will be eight nominees in each of these categories. The eligibility period for the 69th annual Grammy Awards ran from Aug. 31, 2025, through Aug. 28, 2026.

The field of likely album of the year contenders hasn’t changed much since we first previewed them in July. I bumped Don Toliver’s Octane up from “Within Reach” to the top tier, taking the place of RAYE’s This Music May Contain Hope., which is competing with a more commercially successful album by another classy English pop-soul singer, Olivia Dean. Both albums could make it, of course, but with many strong albums vying for just eight slots on the final ballot, something’s gotta give.

Paul McCartney and Taylor Swift are both going for history. If McCartney is nominated for The Boys of Dungeon Lane, it will be his record-extending 10th career nomination in this category. If Swift is nominated for The Life of a Showgirl, it will be her eighth nod in this category — more than anyone else in Grammy history for strictly solo albums. Swift is a better bet: She has been nominated in this category with each of her last four regular studio albums. McCartney was last nominated here in 2006.

We show the deluxe editions of hit albums by Noah Kahan and Rosalía because those are the ones that were entered in the Grammy competition.

A total of 683 albums were on the eligibility list in this category. Here are our best guesses for who will be nominated — listed in alphabetical order, followed by a shortlist of other possibilities, also listed in alphabetical order.

Dean, the reigning Grammy winner for best new artist, is among 16 artists who are set to announce the Grammy nominations on a livestream on Nov. 16. Does that mean Recording Academy insiders already know she’s going to sweep the nominations? I doubt it. But they probably suspect she will. This is the English singer’s second studio album. Dean produced it alongside Julian Bunetta, Leon Michels and John Ryan, among others. Bunetta and Ryan were both nominated in this category for work with Sabrina Carpenter in 2025, with Ryan also being nominated for work with the singer in 2026. Michels was nominated for work with Mary J. Blige and Lizzo, both in 2023.

This is Kahan’s fourth studio album and his first to top the Billboard 200. This would be his first nomination in this category. He was up for best new artist at the 2024 ceremony, but lost to Victoria Monét. He co-produced the album with Gabe Simon and Aaron Dessner. The latter was nominated in this category for three Taylor Swift albums: folklore (which won), evermore and The Tortured Poets Department.

This is vying to become the first album by a core country artist to land an album of the year nod since Kacey Musgraves’ Golden Hour, which won in 2019. Langley co-produced this, her sophomore album, with Miranda Lambert and Ben West. If Langley is nominated here, she won’t be the first Ella to land an album of the year nod: Ella Fitzgerald was nominated at the inaugural Grammy ceremony in 1959 for Ella Fitzgerald Sings the Irving Berlin Song Book.

If The Romantic, which Mars co-produced with D’Mile, is nominated here, it will be Mars’ first album to both top the Billboard 200 and land an album of the year nod. Two Mars albums — Doo-Wops & Hooligans and 24K Magic — were nominated in this category (24K Magic won), but neither of them reached No. 1.

Rodrigo’s first two albums, SOUR and GUTS, were nominated here. Her third album seems sure to follow suit, which would make Rodrigo the first artist to be nominated with her first three studio albums since Billie Eilish. Daniel Nigro produced all three of Rodrigo’s albums. you seem pretty sad for a girl so in love is vying to become the second album with a 10-word title to land an album of the year nod, following Lana Del Rey’s Did You Know That There’s a Tunnel Under Ocean Blvd.

Billboard 200 peak: No. 1 (four weeks so far)

Last year, when the Recording Academy invited members of the Latin Recording Academy to join its ranks, nearly 1,000 took the organization up on the offer. Their votes helped Bad Bunny’s DeBÍ TiRAR MáS FOToS become the first Spanish-language album to win album of the year. This year, Rosalía could become the first woman artist to land an album of the year nod with a Spanish-language album. Rosalía co-produced the album, her fourth studio set.

As previously noted, Swift’s last four studio albums (excluding rerecordings) were nominated in this category. If The Life of a Showgirl also makes it, she’ll become just the second artist in Grammy history (following her one-time collaboration partner Kendrick Lamar) to be nominated with five consecutive studio albums. Swift produced her 12th studio album with Max Martin and Shellback. The album has spent 15 weeks atop the Billboard 200, just two weeks shy of Swift’s personal best, which she set with The Tortured Poets Department. Swift’s five pre-Showgirl albums with the longest runs at No. 1 all received album of the year nods.

Toliver’s fifth studio album was his first to top the Billboard 200. It spawned a pair of top 20 Hot 100 hits, “Body” and “E85.” Oliver was nominated in this category as a featured artist on Kanye West’s Donda five years ago, but this would be his first nomination as a lead artist. Toliver was among the producers of the album, which features Travis Scott, Yeat and Rema, among others. Last year, three rap albums were nominated in this category. This appears to be the rap album with the strongest chance of making it this year.

Phoebe Bridgers, Lost Weekend; Brandi Carlile, Returning to Myself; Charli xcx, Music, Fashion, Film; Ariana Grande, Petal; Kehlani, Kehlani; Steve Lacy, Oh yeah?; Madonna, Confessions II; Paul McCartney, The Boys of Dungeon Lane; Kacey Musgraves, Middle of Nowhere; RAYE, This Music May Contain Hope.; Harry Styles, Kiss All the Time. Disco, Occasionally.; Sienna Spiro, Visitor; Tame Impala, Deadbeat

Publishers Quarterly: Sony Music Publishing Makes History Again in Q3

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The publisher is the first company to surpass the 40% market share milestone, with a big assist from Ella Langley's "Choosin' Texas."

This year, Sony Music Publishing keeps topping its own record-breaking performances as measured by Billboard’s 20-year-long history of ranking music publishers by market share for hit songs.

After scoring the highest-ever market share for the Hot 100 in the second quarter with 37.32%, Sony has now become the first publisher ever to surpass the 40% market share milestone with 42.86% in the just-completed third quarter, setting a new record for that chart.

That performance makes Sony the top-ranked publisher for the Hot 100 chart, while it was also the No. 1 publisher on the Top Radio Airplay chart, with a 36.02% tally, thus keeping its No. 1 streak going on both charts for the sixth consecutive quarter.

To accomplish that feat, Sony claimed 76 songs on the Hot 100 chart and 74 songs on the Radio Airplay Songs list, including Ella Langley’s “Choosin’ Texas,” which finished at No. 1 and No. 2, respectively. Also helping Sony maintain its No. 1 ranking was its role as the administrator for the top songwriter on the Radio Airplay chart, Shane “sombr” Boose, who placed three songs on that chart, including the No. 30 tune “Back To Friends.” Perhaps the most astounding part of Sony’s performance on the Hot 100 publisher rankings this quarter is that it beat out the No. 2 publisher by 25 percentage points.

Coming in second place this quarter was Warner Chappell Music, which turned in a 17.86% market share on the Hot 100 rankings. On the Top Radio Airplay rankings, Warner Chappell was also the No. 2-ranked publisher with a stronger showing, where it tallied a 23.07% market share. On the Top Radio Airplay song list, Warner Chappell snared a stake in 55 songs, with its top song being the No. 3 tune, Bruno Mars’ “Risk It All.” It also had 44 songs on the Hot 100 chart, with its top song, Ariana Grande’s “hate that I made you love me,” landing at No. 4.

This marks the third consecutive quarter that Warner Chappell ranked at No. 2 on both charts. For the 12th consecutive quarter, Warner Chappell was also the top-ranked publisher on the Country Radio Airplay chart with a 33.12% share.

Similarly, for the third consecutive quarter, Universal Music Publishing Group, Kobalt and BMG ranked, respectively, No. 3, No. 4 and No. 5 on both charts.

In placing third, UMPG tallied a 12.31% share on the Hot 100 rankings, with a stake in 39 songs. On the Radio Airplay chart, its share was 11.33% thanks to 42 song placements. Its top song on both charts was “hate that I made you love me,” which was No. 4 on the Hot 100 and No. 5 on Top Radio Airplay. Both those performances were down from the second quarter, when UMPG had 16.55% and 43 songs on the Hot 100 and 12.45% and 44 songs on Top Radio Airplay. UMPG also benefited from having the top songwriter on the Hot 100 chart, Drake, who placed six songs there.

In the third quarter, Kobalt had a 10.16% share on the Hot 100 and a 9.15% share on Top Radio Airplay, thanks to 36 song placements on the former chart and 34 on the latter. Its top-performing song on both charts was “Choosin’ Texas.”

When compared to the prior quarter, Kobalt’s Hot 100 third-quarter performance was an improvement from the second quarter’s 9.30% market share and 32 song placements. On the Radio Airplay rankings, Kobalt’s performance was down slightly from the 9.38% share it recorded in the second quarter. The publisher’s song count continued to hold steady across both quarters.

Rounding out the top five on the publisher rankings, BMG improved its performance from its second-quarter showing on both charts, with a 7.54% share on Top Radio Airplay and 5.87% on the Hot 100.  In both instances, its performance was around two percentage points greater than the prior quarter, when its respective market share was 5.61% and 3.73%. Likewise, its song count improved on both charts  in the third quarter, with 22 song placements on Top Radio Airplay (versus 12 songs in the second quarter) and 19 songs on the Hot 100 (versus four songs in Q2).

In the third quarter, BMG’s top song on the Hot 100 chart was the No. 9 track, Olivia Dean’s “Man I Need,” while “Risk It All” was its top track on Top Radio Airplay.

On the Hot 100, Walt Disney Music landed at No. 6 with a 2.95% share thanks to its stake in Taylor Swift’s “I Knew It, I Knew You,” the No. 3 song on that chart. In the previous quarter, Walt Disney Music had a 1.21% Hot 100 share due to the same song, which was then ranked at No. 30. Disney also landed the No. 6 spot on Top Radio Airplay with a higher market share score of 3.48%, as the Swift song attained an even higher placement on that chart — the coveted No. 1 spot. Helping Disney attain a higher market share on Radio Airplay was having a stake in a second song on that chart, the No. 47 tune, Chris Brown‘s “It Depends” featuring Bryson Tiller.

Me Gusta Music came in at No. 7 on both charts, the same slot it held on each in the second quarter. However, this time it boasted slightly higher market shares and song counts. On Top Radio Airplay, it had a 2.51% share due to six song placements, including the No. 11 song, Luke Combs’ “Be By You.” That was an improvement from the previous quarter, when it had five song placements and 2.41% on Radio Airplay. On the Hot 100, its 2.19% with six song placements — including the No. 8 song, Ella Langley and Morgan Wallen’s “I Can’t Love You Anymore” — improved on its 2.01% share and four song placements in the second quarter.

Songs of Spirit finished at No. 9 on the Hot 100, where the publisher had a 0.80% share with two song placements; while on the Radio Airplay chart, it was No. 10 with a 0.71% share with four song placements. Its top song on both charts was Ella Langley’s “Be Her,” which finished at No. 7 on the former and No. 33 on the latter.

In the bottom half of the Hot 100 rankings, two publishers made their debut on the chart: Endurance Music Group, which placed at No. 8 with a 0.88% showing, thanks to a stake in two songs, including “Be Her”; and Empire, which held down the No. 10 slot with a 0.52% showing thanks to a share in two songs, including the No. 58 tune, Shaboozey’s “Cowgirl.”

Rounding out the Radio Airplay rankings, Reservoir’s 0.87% gave it the No. 8 spot, thanks to six song placements, including the No. 12 song, Kehlani’s “Folded;” while Big Machine Music’s 0.74% and three song placements landed it in the No. 9 spot, due to its top song, Luke Bryan’s “Country and She Knows It,” landing at No. 27. In the prior quarter, Big Machine was lower in the rankings on that chart at No. 10, but higher in market share with a 1.34% slice.

Methodology: For the Top 10 Publishers Top Radio Airplay chart, percentage calculations were based upon the overall top 100 detected songs from 2,392  U.S. radio stations electronically monitored by Mediabase 24 hours a day, seven days a week during the period of July 1 to Sept. 30, 2026. For Top 10 Publishers Hot 100 Songs, percentage calculations were based upon the top 100 songs ranked by Billboard Hot 100 points calculated from digital sales, streaming and Mediabase-tracked radio airplay detections during the period of June 26 to Sept. 24 and reflecting the issue dates of July 11, 2026, through Oct. 3, 2026. Publisher information for musical works on both charts has been identified by the Harry Fox Agency. A “publisher” is defined as an administrator, copyright owner and/or controlling party.
